# Changelog

All notable changes to the Classroom Token Hub project will be documented in this file.

The format is based on [Keep a Changelog](https://keepachangelog.com/en/1.0.0/),
and this project follows semantic versioning principles.


## [Unreleased]

### Accessibility

- **Full WCAG 2.1 A/AA coverage of all 88 real page templates (2026-09-23)** — Completed the axe-core/Playwright accessibility campaign started in `tests/test_axe_app_pages.py`: added `tests/simulated/`, a persistent production-sourced world database, to reach the ten remaining pages that need a real pre-existing claim, issue, insurance policy, announcement, or teacher-recovery row rather than a freshly-provisioned classroom (`admin_process_claim.html`, `admin_view_issue.html`, `sysadmin_view_issue.html`, `student_file_claim.html`, `student_view_policy.html`, `admin_announcement_form.html` edit mode, `student_detail.html`, `admin_recovery_prepare.html`, `admin_recovery_status.html`, `student_verify_recovery.html`). All ten pass with zero violations. Seven further templates were confirmed to have no live route rendering them at all (`admin_view_student_policy.html`, three dead `student/recovery/*.html` leaves, and three retired sysadmin log pages) and are excluded from accessibility scope as dead code rather than tested.

- **Public monitoring history and restored status layout (2026-09-21)** — Restored the original public-page icon wordmark and compact mockup cards, separated platform measurements from teacher-facing estimates, and retained keyboard/touch history disclosures with daily text tables. Fixed warning-label contrast and aligned result bars when questions wrap. Final validation: 209 targeted tests passed, including 20 required accessibility tests. Twelve rendered desktop/mobile state combinations passed; after the final alignment correction, six populated/healthy/severe combinations were repeated with zero axe findings, no overflow, aligned bars, and correct platform outcomes. Original/canonical hero branding was visually and structurally compared. See `docs/ops/STATUS_SIMPLIFICATION_VALIDATION.md` for scope and limitations; this is not full accessibility certification.

### Operations

- **Public request monitoring (2026-09-21)** — Separate objective request counts, 404/500/5xx rates and p80/p95 from operator impact notices. Preserve internal integrity verification while replacing public feature certification with bounded measured-window history and explicit monitoring gaps.

- **Cloudflare Access replaces application maintenance gating (2026-09-21)** — Removed the application maintenance flag, page, query-token and persistent-session bypasses, bypass layout fields/banner styling, and toggle workflow. Cloudflare Access now owns restricted admission for both prelaunch and operational work; application sign-in and capability checks remain required. Updated runbooks and user guidance, and added the supplied logo at `github-pages/assets/cth-logo.svg` for the Access branding URL. Accessibility scope is removal of the maintenance screen and bypass banners, plus the updated help link; no accessibility improvement is claimed. Validation results and remaining coverage limits are recorded in the PR.

- **Feature-health review completion (2026-09-20)** — Defined separate readiness/correctness reduction keys and a distinct capability projection, explicit per-feature freshness limits with independently aged audit-lineage proofs, and collector-owned bounded transport metadata. These are governing documentation changes; feature evaluators are not claimed implemented or deployed.

- **Feature-health review clarifications (2026-09-19)** — The governing contracts now record evidence source, freshness class, and receipt-time staleness without treating a historical freshness result as current. External observations can transport CTH-produced results while preserving their source and owning evaluator version separately from collector protocol version. Login, attendance, and roster retain separate readiness and correctness dimensions; Productivity owns attendance-session verification.

- **App evidence keeps its own clock and provenance (2026-09-19)** — The status collector now records the app-reported check time for each measured signal and distinguishes app-origin results from its own outside-in HTTP reachability check. Persisted observations also carry their approved freshness class, receipt-time staleness state, and a separate evaluator version when a registered feature result exists. A stale feature result becomes Unknown without hiding a fresh database result; unregistered feature checks remain Unknown. The health payload and collector must be rolled out together; mixed versions fail closed until both are deployed.

- **Feature status requires affirmative evidence (2026-09-19)** — The Operations status contract now distinguishes naturally observed feature activity from synthetic probes. Login, Attendance, Payroll, Roster, and Classroom Economy can report an observed-runtime-integrity PASS only when recent real activity and all registered execution, validity, lineage, and reconciliation checks are fresh and affirmative; proven failures remain failures, while idle or incomplete evidence remains UNKNOWN. External status infrastructure receives bounded results only and no classroom credentials or tenant data. This is a governing contract, not a claim that the evaluators are implemented or deployed.

### Fixed

- **Hall-pass approval, timed-out pages and token rotation (2026-09-21)** — Three defects from the second live-test round, all of the same shape: the mechanism was present and correct and something upstream made it unreachable. **A teacher could not approve a hall pass at all.** The route opened `FEATContext("FEAT-PROD-002")` and then called `record_hall_pass_log`, whose `@requires_feat_context` decorator *opens* a context rather than asserting one; nesting is forbidden, so every approval raised `FEATContextError`, which neither handler caught, and the student could not be let out of the room. `/tap` documents this exact trap in its own docstring three hundred lines below the route that committed it. **A timed-out teacher got a bare 9-byte "Not Found"** on payroll, store, banking, rent, insurance and hall pass — blueprint `before_request` hooks run before the view and therefore before `admin_required`, so the fail-closed capability gate answered before the login redirect could. It was evaluating feature authority before authentication had been decided and could not tell "your session expired" from "this class has rent turned off"; the page's own background poll meanwhile received the correct `401`. **The hall-pass verification link could never be rotated** — the fetch omitted `X-CSRFToken`, so Flask-WTF rejected every attempt with 400 before the route ran, disabling the documented remedy for a leaked link; the refusal then arrived as HTML, an unguarded `r.json()` threw into `.catch()`, and the page announced "Failed to contact the server" about a server that had answered clearly.

- **A missing CSRF token can no longer ship unnoticed (2026-09-21)** — The suite runs with `WTF_CSRF_ENABLED=False`, so an absent `X-CSRFToken` is invisible to all ~3,400 tests and appears only in a browser, as a 400 the route never sees: the feature is disabled, not degraded. `AppCore.csrfFetch` already existed and nine of ten POST sites used it; nothing stopped a tenth from calling `fetch` directly. A source-level guard now reports any state-changing `fetch` without a token, matching parentheses through strings and nested braces so a neighbouring protected call cannot vouch for an unprotected one — the near miss that mattered, since the hall-pass defect sat in a file where four other POSTs did attach the header. It found a second live instance immediately: **sysadmin passkey deletion was unreachable**, sending `DELETE` to a route registered for `POST` (405 before CSRF was consulted) *and* carrying no token, so fixing either alone would have left it broken. The teacher-side equivalent had both right; two implementations of one operation had drifted.

- **Class-scoped deadlines resolve in the class timezone, and CI now says so (2026-09-21)** — `first_rent_due_date` was parsed with a naive `strptime` and stored as midnight UTC, which west of Greenwich is 17:00 the *previous* local day: a teacher who entered October 20 got a cycle boundary on the 19th, and the two surfaces disagreed in public — the teacher page printed the 20th and the student page the 19th for the same obligation, with the system acting on the student's. Both directions are fixed, the read as well as the write, because formatting the stored instant in UTC shows the previous local day for any class *east* of Greenwich. This was the third site of a defect class named in `SPEC-TIME-001 §CLE` all along, so the durable fix is the tenth policy guardrail rather than a fourth patch: an AST rule reporting any of nine class-scoped temporal columns assigned from `strptime`, `combine`, `today`, `now`, `utcnow` or `fromtimestamp`. Proved against the real file — with `admin.py` at the commit that shipped the defect, CI reports `admin.py:5385`. Also fixed: the **rent pricing recommendation was withheld until rent was already priced**, so a first-time teacher read "insufficient data" directly above a printout of every operand it needed, from two independent causes each masking the other; and a **configuration gap became a 500** once the second of those was corrected, because `calculate_cwi` returns `None` when expected weekly hours are unset and the validator did not handle it.

- **Attendance immutability is enforced by the database (2026-09-21)** — `DOM-PROD-001` states it four times and the database enforced none of it: `attendance_sessions` carried no triggers and a direct `UPDATE` succeeded silently. `ledger_transaction` — the output — could not be rewritten, while the input that justifies every dollar in it could. `UPDATE` is now refused outright; `DELETE` is gated rather than forbidden, because unlike `audit_events` these rows are legitimately removed when a class or teacher account is destroyed, and a blanket guard would break account destruction. The teardown path declares itself with `SET LOCAL`, so the permission expires with its transaction and cannot leak onto a pooled connection and silently disarm the guard — a test asserts that expiry, since a guard that looks present but is not is worse than none.

- **Payroll reversal has a surface, so the only documented remedy is reachable (2026-09-21)** — `DOM-PROD-001 §187` names payroll reversal as the remedy for an attendance row that produced a wrong payroll outcome, and `record_payroll_reversal` appeared exactly once in the repository: its own definition. No route, service or test reached it, while the payroll template already rendered a `REVERSAL` badge and the view model carried a hardcoded `can_reverse: False`. A student could raise an attendance dispute, a teacher could acknowledge it, and nothing could remedy it — making the student whole meant a manual credit, which records itself as `manual_credit` rather than as a correction, so the money moved and the record did not say why. The control drives `FEAT-PROD-003`, not the bare command, which is what keeps the two together: the reversal inherits the original's `policy_version_id` and posts the negation of the linked transaction, so the pair nets to exactly zero and the original entry survives. It lives on Payroll rather than on the support ticket, because a teacher can find a payroll mistake with no ticket in existence and Support must not acquire a Productivity remedy.

- **Surfaces that named things wrongly or not at all (2026-09-21)** — The student export labelled `section` as "Block", retired v1 vocabulary no teacher-facing template still renders. A **stale second tab** is now detectable: class context is one server-side value per teacher, so switching class in one tab changes the other tab's context while it keeps displaying the old class — isolation held (writes are scoped and skip out-of-scope seats, verified live against a real stale tab) but a manual credit reported "applied to 0 student(s)" styled as *success*, naming no cause, so the reasonable conclusion was that the feature was broken. Pages now stamp the class they were rendered for, the poll every admin page already makes returns the active class, and a mismatch raises a banner on focus. Seven `print()` calls in the canonical context resolver, firing on identity-resolution failures, now use structured logging with the correlation id and class context the app attaches.

- **The rent page says which policy students are actually on (2026-09-21)** — `rent_settings` is append-only and a bill cycle freezes the `policy_uuid` it was created under, so a rent change saved while a cycle is open does not bill anyone until `reconcile_rent` mints the successor cycle. That deferral is deliberate — a teacher cannot raise rent on a period students are already living through — but the admin page rendered the newest `IN_USE` row under the heading **"Current Rent Configuration"**, pre-filled the settings form from it, and flashed "Rent settings updated successfully!". Each statement is true in isolation and the page as a whole was wrong: a teacher who raised rent from $50 to $75 saw $75 echoed back as current while every student was still assessed $50, with nothing anywhere indicating the difference. The two surfaces had been asking different questions all along — `/admin/rent-settings` resolves `get_rent_settings` (newest `IN_USE`), `/student/rent` resolves the open cycle's frozen policy — and only diverge after a mid-cycle save, which is precisely when the teacher is looking. A standing notice now names the terms in force, the terms pending, and the date the pending ones start; the Overview heading reads "Saved" rather than "Current" while a policy is deferred; and the save flash states the same thing instead of an unqualified success. The effective date is the cycle's `next_assessment_at` — the instant the successor is minted and binds the then-current policy — not `cycle_boundary_at`, and it renders as a **class-local** date through CLE, since a UTC reading of that instant shows the previous day for any class west of Greenwich (the display form of the same defect fixed in the class-local dates entry below). A terminal cycle, which mints no successor, gets its own wording rather than an effective date it will never reach. Surfaced by live testing: the invariant refused an operator trying to reach a payable rent bill, and the page gave them no way to understand why.

- **Advanced payroll rate no longer divides by 60 on every re-save (2026-09-19)** — `PayrollSettings.pay_rate` is canonically dollars per minute, while the Advanced settings form asks for dollars per the teacher's chosen unit and converts on save. The display side never converted back: it rendered the raw per-minute figure against the chosen unit, so a class configured at $1.50/hour — stored as $0.025/minute — showed "$0.02" (two-decimal formatting rounds half to even). Because that same value pre-populates the pay-rate input, opening and re-saving the settings page stored $0.02/hour, or $0.00033/minute: **a 75-fold cut for that example**, silently, on a financial setting. The factor is not fixed — the missing conversion accounts for 60× on its own, and rounding the displayed value moves it either way — which is part of why the corruption was hard to notice. The two directions now share one table of exact integer second-ratios (`SECONDS_PER_TIME_UNIT` in `app/services/payroll/builders.py`), imported by the route rather than written out at both ends, which is how they came to disagree; a test asserts the display→save round trip is exact for every unit and that the route uses the shared helper. Surfaced by review of the rate-unit wording fix below.

- **Automatic payroll advances by local calendar days (2026-09-19)** — `next_payroll_date` advanced by `timedelta(days=N)`, which preserves the clock time in UTC rather than in the class's timezone. With the anchor now stored as class-local midnight, a DST transition inside the interval turned that into 23:00 the previous local day (or 01:00 the same day), and every later run inherited the shift — so the schedule would drift onto the wrong local date twice a year, the same defect the class-local anchor was introduced to remove. The advance now preserves the occurrence's local wall-clock components on the target date, with explicit policy for the two edge cases: an ambiguous target time (the clock reads 01:30 twice on fall-back) takes the first, still-DST occurrence, and a nonexistent one (02:30 never happens on spring-forward) takes the smallest forward shift onto a time that does exist, so 02:30 becomes 03:30 rather than 03:00. Both are chosen by comparing the candidate instants rather than by passing an `is_dst` flag: in Europe/Dublin the tz database models winter as *negative* DST, so `is_dst=True` returns the later instant there — the opposite of Los Angeles — and a flag-based rule silently inverts for Irish classes. An intermediate version added the *elapsed* UTC duration since local midnight instead, which is not the same quantity — on a 25-hour day an occurrence at 23:30 local is 24h30m past midnight, so the run landed on the following local date, reintroducing the defect in a narrower case. The local time of day is preserved rather than normalised to midnight, because `next_payroll_date` falls back to `created_at` for classes that never set a first pay date and forcing midnight would have moved their established run time.

- **Live-test surfaces: empty rosters, unscheduled payroll, error copy and the service-worker bypass (2026-09-19)** — The display and wording findings from the first live deployment, four of which stated something false. **An empty roster no longer reports "all seats claimed"** — "every unclaimed seat is claimed" is vacuously true of no seats at all, so a brand-new class was shown the one state it certainly was not in; an empty roster is now a third state with its own label rather than a degenerate case of the second. **The dashboard's "Next Payroll" reads the configured schedule.** It previously projected a date from the last run and, with no runs to project from, invented "the next Friday", consulting `payroll_settings` at no point — so a class with no payroll configured was shown a confident date for a run that would never happen, beneath a real cost estimate that made the invented date read as equally real. It now reads the row the scheduler actually fires on and renders "Not scheduled" when there is none. **Payroll settings say "This class", not "Global Default"** — v1 vocabulary from when settings spanned a teacher's blocks, which a teacher running several classes would read as applying to all of them. The rate unit is singularised ("$1.50/minute", not "/minutes"), the stray space before the schedule comma is gone, and the Pay Simulator now states that rounding is an Advanced-mode behaviour while Simple mode pays worked time to the second — `_round_billable_seconds` returns early unless the mode is advanced, so a teacher reading the rounding note while previewing Advanced would otherwise carry it into a Simple-mode class that pays partial minutes. Hardcoded "midnight PST" in the daily-limit copy now refers to the class's own timezone. **The app can link to its own status page**: the `STATUS_PAGE_URL` allowlist named only UptimeRobot and silently rejected `status.classroomtokenhub.com`, omitting the link with no error anywhere; it stays an exact-prefix allowlist, so an attacker who can set the variable still cannot plant an arbitrary destination. **Students are told what happened rather than which domain refused** — a failed purchase rendered the FEAT's developer diagnostic verbatim ("Purchase denied by Ledger", with a raw reason code or the literal word "unknown" interpolated into the ledger branch); the student-facing sentence is now derived from `error_code` at the presentation boundary and the diagnostic goes to the log, where it is useful. **The service worker's auth bypass names real prefixes**: it excluded `/system-admin`, which is not and never was a registered blueprint prefix — the sysadmin blueprint is `/sysadmin` — so every sysadmin route fell through to the caching strategies in a list whose stated purpose is multi-tenancy safety. A guard now checks the declared prefixes against the registered blueprints, with a mutation proof feeding it the exact shipped misspelling. The Cloudflare origin check now prefers `X-CF-Edge-IP` (the pre-rewrite peer) and falls back to `X-Real-IP` where that header is absent, because `real_ip_header CF-Connecting-IP` rewrites `X-Real-IP` to the end client — the check could never succeed while real-ip rewriting was on, and rewriting is load-bearing for rate limiting, so the two features had been competing for one header. Static-asset caching and icon-font subsetting remain open: both need groundwork (asset versioning, a build step) that should not ride along with a wording pass.

- **Live-test defects: immediate use, credential boundary, hall-pass path and class-local dates (2026-09-19)** — Five functional defects found during the first live deployment, none of which a 2,997-test suite had caught. **Immediate-use items are now consumed at sale.** `SPEC-STORE-001` §III defines `IMMEDIATE_USE` as "granted and consumed in the same action", but `instant_use` was a caller-supplied argument defaulting to `False` that the only purchase route never set, leaving such items sitting unexercised in My Items behind a "Use Now" button the specification does not permit — while the purchase modal simultaneously promised the student they would not need to redeem it later. It is now derived inside `FEAT-STOR-001` from the resolved product's `entitlement_type` and removed from the public signature, so no call site can get it wrong again. **The redemption credential matches the normative matrix.** FEAT-IDEN-002's credential boundary assigns "using an entitlement except hall passes" to the passphrase; the Use Item modal was labelled "PIN" with a numeric keypad while the server verified the passphrase, so a student who typed what the label asked for was refused, and on a phone the field raised the wrong keyboard for the credential it actually wanted. The field, its help text and the surrounding guidance now all say passphrase. **Hall passes are no longer requested from the Store.** Exercising a pass marks a student out of an active work session (DOM-PROD-001 records `reason_code = hall_pass` on the attendance timeline), so the request belongs to the dashboard Break flow, which refuses when the seat holds no pass and when the latest attendance event is not `active`. The Store's second path enforced neither, so a student who had never clocked in — or had already finished for the day — could request one; it now refuses and points at the Break button. Purchased passes still credit the hall-pass balance, so nothing is lost. **That lawful path now requires the PIN**, which the same matrix assigns to hall-pass use as its explicit exception; it previously verified no credential at all, so anyone holding a student's session could spend a pass off that seat's balance and place the student on the attendance timeline as out of the room. **Teacher-entered dates resolve in the class timezone.** `first_pay_date`, store `activation_at`, `auto_delist_date` and `collective_goal_expires_at` combined the chosen calendar date with UTC midnight, so for every class west of Greenwich the anchor landed on the previous local day and automatic payroll fired a day early. They now resolve through CLE, the only authority that reads `ClassEconomy.class_timezone` — `_end_of_day_utc` had routed through SLE, which reads as timezone-aware but returns UTC unconditionally. The start-date validator compared against the server's `date.today()`, telling a teacher in the Americas after late afternoon that their own current date was "before today"; it now compares against the class's local date. Regression tests in `tests/test_live_test_defect_regressions.py` were each confirmed to fail against the deployed SHA before the fixes; the hall-pass tests first provision a pass and an active session so a refusal is evidence of the credential check rather than of an empty balance, and the payroll test forces a class timezone matching neither UTC nor the developer's host, without which it passed on a Pacific machine and failed only on the UTC server.

### Status service

- Resolve selected open issues through version-bound checkboxes rather than free-text resolution references. Resolution events and current projections are written atomically; stale, missing, or already resolved selections reject the entire submission. History and unselected issues are preserved.

### Interface

- **Unknown feature status explains its evidence gap (2026-09-19)** — Status cards now say “Not recently verified” and explain that insufficient recent evidence is not a detected failure. A native disclosure exposes the bounded reason and last recorded check time when available, without requiring hover or implying that idle activity is healthy.

- **Status summaries share one freshness clock (2026-09-19)** — The hero and service cards use the same request timestamp, keeping their result and last-checked text consistent at the freshness boundary. Next-update times use readable UTC formatting when a timezone is supplied; values without a timezone say so explicitly. The collector deployment test now rejects duplicate commands even when their step names match.

- **The student class-selection page loads the stylesheets it renders against (2026-09-18)** — `student_select_class_context.html` declares its own document and loaded only `fonts.css` and `style.css`. `style.css` consumes design tokens but does not import them, so every `var(--…)` resolved to nothing, and without Bootstrap the page's `card`, `container` and spacing utilities had no rules either — including the `alert_card` macro the page had started rendering. It now loads vendored Bootstrap and `tokens.css` ahead of `style.css`, matching its sibling `admin_select_class_context.html`. This was the last standalone page missing the stack after Bootstrap was vendored; `tests/dom/platform/test_vendored_frontend_assets.py` now asserts that every template declaring a full document loads both layers.


- **Every inline alert is a card (2026-09-16)** — Rounded Bootstrap `.alert` boxes are retired across teacher, student, sysadmin, auth, error, docs, and help pages. Each alert now renders as a card with a semantic border, a colored header carrying an icon and a contextual title, and the explanation in the body, matching the recovery-readiness warning on Student Management. Templates use the `alert_card` macro in `templates/macros/cards.html` (now with `role`, `id`, `dismissible`, and heading options); scripts use its DOM twin `AppCore.buildAlertCard` in `static/js/app-core.js`, which sets body text without parsing HTML. Flash messages outside toasts become dismissible cards titled by category, and docs `> [!NOTE]`-style callouts render as cards. Announcement priority maps to a card level through `Announcement.get_priority_level()`, replacing `get_priority_class()`. The legacy `.alert` CSS overrides are removed. Toasts are unchanged.

### Support

- **The migration chain no longer creates the seat foreign keys it forbids (2026-09-18)** — `e3c3d4e5f6a7` created `fk_issues_actor_public_id_seats` and `a5e5f6a7b8c9` created `fk_actor_request_trace_seat`, and `d9e1f3a5b7c9` dropped both a few revisions later because INV-ARC-021 §V.7 permits cross-domain foreign keys only to `class_id`, `seat_id` and `user_id`. Building a constraint the same chain then removes was self-contradictory, and for `issues` it was unsafe: the request-trace revision deletes orphaned rows before constraining its column, but nothing reconciled `issues` first, so a single legacy ticket whose `actor_public_id` no longer matched a live seat — an unclaimed seat holding an earlier claimant's tickets, for instance — failed the upgrade before it could reach the revision that removes the key. `d9e1f3a5b7c9`'s own downgrade already refused to restore the key when such rows existed, so the hazard was understood at the far end of the chain but not the near one. Neither key is created now; `e3c3d4e5f6a7` keeps its `class_public_id` NOT NULL change and `a5e5f6a7b8c9` keeps its orphan sweep, mismatch check and the permitted `class_id` key. `d9e1f3a5b7c9`'s upgrade stays a defensive drop so databases that applied the earlier forms converge, and its downgrade is now a no-op rather than rebuilding a schema no upgrade path produces. Seat deletion already removed these rows explicitly (`app/utils/student_deletion.py`, DOM-SUP-001 §X), so nothing depended on the cascade, and no migration between the creations and the drop deletes seats. All three revisions are unmerged, so no SOP-DB-001 exception applies. `tests/dom/support/test_no_cross_domain_seat_foreign_keys.py` guards the whole migration corpus rather than these three revisions. Two existing tests asserted the transient cascade — `test_seat_cascade_migration_upgrade_and_downgrade` and `test_trace_migration_removes_detached_rows_and_installs_cascades` — and were pinning behaviour the final contract forbids rather than a requirement; both now assert that the revision installs no key to `seats`, that a ticket or trace outlives its seat for the explicit sweep to remove, and that the permitted `class_id` cascade still takes traces with their class.


- **Teacher announcements save again (2026-09-17)** — The announcement list, edit, delete and toggle pages filtered on the removed `Announcement.user_id` and returned a server error. They now scope by the teacher's verified class only. Behind that error, every announcement write flushed outside a FEAT and was rolled back, so creating, editing, toggling or deleting an announcement silently failed. The writes now run in the new FEAT-SUP-002, which requires the acting seat to teach the announcement's own class on every write, not only on create. DOM-SUP-001 v1.7 names that FEAT, and FEAT-SUP-001 now describes deletion by public ID rather than a seat foreign-key cascade.
- **Support references seats by public ID only (2026-09-17)** — `issues.actor_public_id` and `actor_request_trace.actor_public_id` no longer carry foreign keys to `seats.public_id`, which INV-ARC-021 §V.7 does not permit across domains. Seat deletion resolves the seat's public ID and deletes its tickets and request traces explicitly, including for unclaimed seats that kept an earlier claimant's tickets; traces still cascade with their class through `class_id`. Ticket writers share-lock the live seat, and the post-response trace writer skips a locked seat instead of waiting. Migration `d9e1f3a5b7c9` drops the keys. DOM-SUP-001 v1.6 and DOM-CORE-002 v1.9 record the lifetime rule and attribute `actor_request_trace` to Support and `recovery_class_challenges` to Identity. The class and teacher destruction commands moved from the admin routes to `app/services/teacher_destruction.py`.
- **Teacher-controlled diagnostic disclosure (2026-09-15)** — Added separate, unchecked permissions for balances, the reported transaction, recent transactions, the student's report, and the class name. Server-side projections enforce these choices on operator list and detail pages. Technical route diagnostics, IP address, and browser details remain available. Correlation packs are now persisted with ticket creation instead of discarded. Every ticket belongs to its originating seat and class; there is no account-level ticket scope. Captured values stay frozen, and database cascades remove the ticket and attached support rows when that seat is deleted. Roster notes are documented as encrypted, class-scoped teacher-entered context without assertions about their contents.

### Obligations

- **Bill-cycle succession is one command with command-identity replay (DOM-OBL-001 v3.1, FEAT-OBL-002 v2.0, 2026-09-24)** — `schedule_next_bill_cycle` (`app/feats/schedule_next_bill_cycle_feat.py`) replaces the rent path's genesis/advance pair. The domain derives the cycle number (`1` for an empty lineage, otherwise `current + 1`), and the request has no cycle number to supply. Succession is lawful only for an empty lineage or a non-terminal latest cycle whose `next_assessment_at` has arrived. This is evaluated through the canonical temporal resolver and exposed as `obligations_service.get_succession_eligibility`, so callers ask the domain instead of reconstructing the rule. Replay is by command identity: new table `obligation_command_reservation` (migration `c4d6e8f0a2b3`, scope `(class_id, command_name, idempotency_key)`, fingerprint of caller intent only) records each executed command and the cycle it produced. Same identity + same terms returns the original cycle; same identity + different terms fails closed; a different command that created the derived cycle first yields `SuccessionConflictError`, and the loser does not re-derive a later cycle. **Fixed:** `advance_bill_cycle` treated any existing `(internal_ref, cycle_number)` row as a successful replay (`check_idempotency_bill_cycle`), so two unrelated commands were indistinguishable; both are removed. Rent reconciliation now creates every cycle, including cycle 1, through succession, with a per-run command identity (`rent-reconcile:{class_id}:{reference instant}` or a caller-supplied key, plus `:after:{evaluated cycle}` per step), and its loop asks the domain for eligibility instead of comparing datetimes. **Deliberately not done:** insurance purchase still creates its cycle 1 through the interim `establish_bill_cycle`, kept only for that caller, until the insurance recurring-premium lifecycle is ratified and its lineage is keyed by `entitlement_id`. DOM-CORE-002 v1.10 and DOM-OBL-001 §VI list the new table. Tests: `tests/dom/obligations/test_bill_cycle_succession.py` (19, including two two-transaction Postgres races: distinct identities conflict without falling forward to N+2; the same identity racing itself replays); mutation-proofed by re-deriving on conflict, treating a same-shape row as a replay, and removing the not-due and terminal checks, each of which turns a test red.

- **An assessment is attributed to the assessment FEAT, not to rent payment (2026-09-19)** — `FEAT-OBLI-001` ("Assess Obligation", contract v1.1) and `FEAT-OBL-001` ("Rent Payment", registry) are different workflows in one domain whose ids differ by one letter, and `app/feats/assess_obligation_feat.py` implemented the first while executing under the second; its own comment cited "FEAT-OBL-001 §V", a section of a document that does not exist. `INV-ARC-000` §VIII.2 derives a request's recorded action from its active FEAT and `feat_code` is stamped from that context, so the entry point was contracted to emit false lineage. The defect was latent rather than live: `execute_assess_obligation` has no production caller — rent and insurance assessment reach `assess_obligation` from inside `FEAT-OBL-002` and `FEAT-OBL-004`, which attribute correctly — and assessments write no Ledger effect, so no `ledger_command_reservation` identity (`UNIQUE(class_id, feat_code, idempotency_key)`) depended on the wrong code. `FEAT-OBLI-001` is now registered as its own Obligations workflow, the entry point is decorated with it, and the citation is corrected. Deliberately not done: renaming either concept, writing the still-missing `FEAT-OBL-001` contract, or touching rent payment — those stay in `docs/TRACKING/FEAT_REGISTRY_RECONCILIATION_2026-09-19.md` as post-launch work. `tests/dom/obligations/test_assessment_feat_attribution.py` pins the attribution, the ASSESSMENT row that must survive re-labelling, and the adjacent surfaces that must not move; the attribution test was watched failing against the old decorator. A docstring in `test_obligations_domain.py` that labelled the assessment tests `FEAT-OBL-001` is corrected to match.

### Class Configuration

- **Class destruction is FEAT-CLASS-006, not the create-a-class FEAT (2026-09-17)** — Destroying a class boundary executed under `FEAT-CLASS-001`, whose contract is class-boundary *creation*: its §III states the workflow "SHALL NOT execute within CanonicalContext because the target Class Boundary does not yet exist", its §VII and §VIII describe rolling back a provisioning transaction, and its §X guarantees "exactly one Class Boundary is **established**". `FEAT-IDEN-007` §Composition designated it for destruction regardless, so two Normative documents disagreed about what that FEAT was and the runtime followed the wrong one. Every request executes a single command path whose domain, capability and action are recorded as part of its observability (INV-ARC-000 §VIII.2), and a FEAT names a user-facing action rather than a bucket of operations on one table (INV-CORE-001) — so "this class universe was destroyed by the create-a-class command" was a false audit statement, not an untidy one. Destruction also has semantics creation has no analogue for: class-scoped data must not survive deletion of its `class_id` (INV-CORE-000 §26, §33) and a principal holding no Seat anywhere cannot exist (DOM-IDEN-005 §V.6, §VI). `FEAT-CLASS-006` ("Destroy class boundary", Class Configuration, blast radius HIGH) is registered and contracted in `docs/FEATURE-EXECUTION/FEAT-CLASS-006_DESTROYING_CLASS_BOUNDARY.md`, and both surfaces that reach it — `POST /admin/join-code/delete` and roster deletion that empties the final roster — now execute it. Its §IV.2 carries the same bound established for teacher destruction: the pre-FEAT read only selects the executor, and the FEAT re-acquires the locks, re-evaluates whether the principal still survives the teardown, and fails closed with no mutation when it would not, which the join-code route surfaces as a 409 instead of a 500. Both destruction paths take the `users` and `classes` locks in one fixed order through a shared helper so they cannot deadlock against each other. `FEAT-CLASS-001` is creation only and now says so in a new §V.A with a matching §X guarantee; the old designation is treated as normative debt exposed by review, not precedent. Retargeted: `FEAT-IDEN-007` §Composition and §Automatic/roster-terminal entry points, `FEAT-IDEN-006` §Roster removal, `MAP-UI-001`, `app/services/teacher_destruction.py`, and the Class Configuration FEAT index. Class creation is unchanged, and `FEAT-IDEN-007` still composes the plain `_destroy_class_scope_rows` command rather than entering `FEAT-CLASS-006`, because a FEAT never executes another FEAT. `tests/dom/class/test_class_destruction_feat_identity.py` covers registry separation, a structural guard that no destruction entry point is *declared* under `FEAT-CLASS-001` (including paths a run never reaches), both surfaces' attribution, audit lineage, the locked refusal, atomicity, and hard-deletion scoping against a surviving sibling class.

### Identity

- **Unclaim renames the seat it is unclaiming (2026-09-18)** — `unclaim_student_seat` wrote the teacher's freshly entered names to the seat's claim material — `claim_first_name_hash`, `claim_last_name_hash`, `roster_fingerprint` — and left the `IdentityProfile` alone. Unclaiming "Ava Chen" as "Robin Vale" therefore left the roster showing Ava Chen while the seat was claimable only as Robin Vale. The teacher reads the roster and directs the student by the name on screen, so the claim fails for a reason neither of them can see. It now writes the display name in the same transaction; notes, balances, transactions, attendance and every other seat-owned fact are untouched. DOM-IDEN-005 v2.2 §Explicit Unclaim records the rule and why: an unclaimed seat's displayed name and its claim key must name the same person, and unclaim is both the only point at which an unclaimed seat's name may lawfully change and the only operation that asks the teacher for names. Editing an unclaimed seat's name stays refused (DOM-IDEN-002 §VIII.7), and a claimed seat's rename still touches the display name alone — safe because a claimed seat has no claim material left to disagree with. FEAT-IDEN-006 §Explicit Unclaim is amended to match. The previous clause — "Require freshly entered names *solely* to regenerate claim hashes; preserve existing profile display names, encrypted notes, and economic/productivity/support facts" — was about not discarding what the seat had accumulated; pinning the display name to a departed claimant was not its intent, and the implementation had followed it literally. `test_unclaim_preserves_display_and_records_then_normal_claim_works` asserted the old behaviour and has been corrected rather than deleted; two tests were added for the roster/claim-key invariant and for notes and money surviving the rename. The unclaim modal no longer promises that “the saved display name … stay[s] unchanged”, which had become false, and the teacher guide documents Unclaim for the first time — it had listed the roster's actions as “Edit and Delete” while the page offered three. DOM-IDEN-005 v2.2 also records why the names must be **entered** rather than derived from the stored profile: `identity_profiles` is display-only identity (INV-ARC-019), its values are decryptable, and regenerating the claim hashes from them would let display-layer metadata backfill the authoritative key that governs who may claim a seat — besides inferring that the teacher wanted the name unchanged, which is the teacher's decision to state, not the backend's to assume. `tests/dom/identity/test_claim_material_is_never_derived.py` holds that direction structurally, since after an unclaim the profile and the claim material agree and no black-box test can tell a derived value from an entered one.

- **The roster-modification FEAT refuses to rename an unclaimed seat (2026-09-18)** — An unclaimed seat's stored name is the key a student matches at claim, and `roster_fingerprint` is what the username derives from. `/admin/student/edit` already refused these renames, but `FEAT-CLASS-002`'s `execute_modify_student` — the `actor_public_id`-based roster-modification path, a separate entry point — validated context, ownership, role and non-empty names without ever checking claim state, then updated the `IdentityProfile` alone. The seat would display the new name while `claim_first_name_hash`/`claim_last_name_hash` stayed on the old one, leaving it claimable only under a name the teacher can no longer see. It now returns `SEAT_NOT_CLAIMED`. Regenerating the hashes instead was considered and rejected: DOM-IDEN-002 §VIII.7 states that "Recovery and display-name edits SHALL NOT regenerate claim artifacts", and DOM-IDEN-005 §Explicit Unclaim puts regeneration at unclaim, from freshly entered names — not as a side effect of an edit, which would also leave the fingerprint and username stale. Removing and re-adding the seat remains the supported correction; claimed seats rename as before, and their cleared claim artifacts stay cleared.


- **Roster deletion is attributed to the FEAT that owns what it destroys (2026-09-17)** — Deleting student seats reaches one of three terminal scopes: seats go and the class survives; the last seats go and the class universe is destroyed with its administrative Seat (DOM-CLASS-001 §Terminal Roster Deletion); or that happens to the teacher's only class and the principal cannot survive it either (DOM-IDEN-005 §V.6, §VI). All three ran inside `FEAT-IDEN-006` — "Provision Student Seat in Existing Class", blast radius MED. The destruction itself was correct and atomic; the attribution was not. `feat_code` on audit and ledger rows and every FEAT-ENTRY record take their value from `get_active_feat_name()`, so an account destruction was recorded under a provisioning FEAT, and the HIGH blast-radius idempotency discipline that `FEAT-IDEN-007` and `FEAT-CLASS-001` carry never applied to it. Dispatch now selects the executor before the FEAT opens, from a preview that holds no lock and authorizes nothing: `FEAT-IDEN-006` for seat removal, `FEAT-CLASS-001` for class destruction, `FEAT-IDEN-007` for account destruction, the latter two taking the same idempotency keys the join-code destruction route uses for the same commands. The locked plan stays authoritative — each executor takes the `users` and `classes` row locks, re-derives the plan, and refuses with a 409 if the terminal scope moved since the preview, so a stale preview cannot borrow a narrow FEAT to perform a wider destruction. The FEAT is still the transaction boundary. `tests/dom/identity/test_roster_deletion_feat_attribution.py` holds each scope's FEAT, the recorded blast radius, the scope-change refusal, and atomicity; the four regression cases were watched failing against the single-FEAT version.
- **Dead deletion helper and shadow-account markup removed (2026-09-17)** — `hard_delete_student_if_orphaned()` and its route wrapper had no callers: roster deletion goes through `remove_student_from_teacher_scope()`. The helper was also self-contradictory, returning early when any seat existed and then querying for the seats it had just ruled out. Separately, `UnclaimedSeatRow.is_teacher` read `getattr(seat, "is_teacher", False)` on a model with no such attribute, so it was always false and the roster's two "Teacher shadow account" blocks were unreachable — v2 has no teacher shadow accounts. SOP-DEP-001 §XIV now names the revisions that cannot be downgraded (`c7a7b8c9d0e1`, `c1a1b2d3e4f5`), so a rollback past them restores the snapshot rather than walking the chain back.
- **Roster bulk actions send seat IDs, and seat deletion locks the seat first (2026-09-17)** — The roster checkbox carried `public_id`, so bulk delete posted UUIDs to a planner that calls `int()` and aborted with 400, and the bulk hall-pass adjuster ran `parseInt()` on a UUID — which silently yields the leading digits, targeting whatever seat that number happens to be. The checkbox now carries the seat id, matching every endpoint it feeds; `public_id` stays where it belongs, in the detail link. Separately, the seat-deletion paths read the seat without a row lock, so a ticket written between the support sweep and the seat delete outlived its seat — `issues` has no foreign key into `seats` (DOM-SUP-001 §X). `lock_seats_for_deletion()` takes `FOR UPDATE` in id order before dependents are collected, in the per-seat, orphaned-principal, single-seat and class-destruction paths, which conflicts with the `FOR SHARE` the issue and trace writers hold.
- **Editing an unclaimed seat is refused server-side (2026-09-17)** — An unclaimed seat's stored name is the key a student matches at claim, and display-name edits do not regenerate claim artifacts (DOM-IDEN-002 §VIII), so renaming one would leave its hashes on the old name while the roster showed the new one — claimable only under a name the teacher can no longer see. The roster has never offered an edit control for these rows, but `/admin/student/edit` checked class and ownership without checking claim state, so a request arriving without the UI could still do it. It now refuses. Removing and re-adding the seat remains the supported correction, and claimed seats edit as before.
- **Store supersession records its author, and a recovery migration reverses completely (2026-09-17)** — Publishing a replacement product version dropped the author: both callers of `supersede_product` omitted `actor_seat_id`, so every store-item edit and every economy rebalance wrote `created_by_seat_id = NULL` — a classroom fact with no owner, which INV-ARC-019 §VII does not permit. The acting teacher seat is now threaded through both paths. Separately, migration `e9c9d0e1f2a3` widened `recovery_requests.resume_new_username` to `Text` on upgrade but its downgrade only dropped the three columns it added, so a rehearsal did not return the schema it started from; the downgrade now restores `String(100)`, clearing the column first because the value is a mid-recovery username that is encrypted from this revision on and may exceed the narrower width.
- **Switch toggles have a visible boundary and state again (2026-09-17)** — Bootstrap's resting control border and switch thumb are both `rgba(0, 0, 0, 0.25)`: 1.84:1 on a white track and 1.30:1 on a dark section header, where the control nearly vanished. Worse, this project set the *checked* fill to `--primary`, which inside a `--primary` card header is 1.00:1 — the Payroll Settings "Advanced Mode" switch showed no track at all, only its white thumb floating in the header. Resting borders and thumbs now use `--neutral-500` (4.76:1 on `--surface`), and inside a dark header the control takes the accent fill (`--secondary` on `--primary`, 4.34:1) with a light boundary (`--text-inverse` at 65%, 5.10:1) and a dark thumb (6.90:1 on the gold). WCAG 1.4.11 requires 3:1 for a control's boundary and its state indicator (INV-ARC-020 §VII.D). `tests/test_outline_warning_contrast.py` computes each ratio from the token and style sheets, and keeps the two failing baselines as evidence.
- **Getting Started's completion card is scaled to its panel (2026-09-17)** — "Required setup complete" is built by the shared alert-card builder, which is a page-level treatment: an h5 heading over 1.25rem of body padding. In the 380px widget, beside 44px checklist rows set in `--text-sm`, it dwarfed everything around it. Alert cards rendered into the widget now use the panel's own type and spacing tokens; the shared builder and macro are unchanged, so page-level alert cards keep their size.
- **Bootstrap is served from the application, at one version (2026-09-17)** — Borders on buttons and switch toggles disappeared intermittently, and collapses, tabs and modals sometimes did nothing. Every layout loaded Bootstrap's CSS and JS from `cdn.jsdelivr.net`; when that request was slow, filtered, or failed, `static/css/style.css` still applied while Bootstrap's component rules did not, so `.btn-outline-*` lost its border (`.btn` supplies `border: 1px solid transparent` and the variant only colours it), `.form-switch` lost its track, and `bootstrap.bundle.min.js` took the interactive components with it. A network behind a filter that blocks the CDN lost the interface outright. Bootstrap 5.3.8 is now vendored at `static/vendor/bootstrap/` and loaded through `url_for`, across 30 templates. This also ends a version split: 5.3.0 was loaded by 13 templates, 5.3.3 by 16 and 5.3.8 by 1, so pages rendered against different Bootstrap builds. `tests/dom/platform/test_vendored_frontend_assets.py` pins local-only loading, one version across the stylesheet and bundle, and the bundle over the bare build, which silently drops Popper-dependent components. Chart.js, EasyMDE, Font Awesome and zxcvbn are still loaded from the CDN and carry the same risk.
- **Student Management roster migrated to a view model, then relaid out (2026-09-17)** — The roster page received thirteen template variables, three of them parallel `by_seat_id` lookup dictionaries the template joined itself, and did its own formatting: `strftime` on a raw timestamp, a `balance < 0` comparison picking a CSS class, and a domain `acquisition_type` choosing a badge colour. `app/services/roster_view_model.py` now builds one frozen `ClassRosterView` with display strings, badge variants and counts already decided, and the route resolves scope and delegates (MAP-UI-001 §VI had this flow as `NEEDS_REWIRE`). This also fixes a column that never worked: `student_rent_privileges_by_seat_id` was initialised empty and never populated, so the Privileges column was permanently blank even for students holding rent perks — the resolver is now actually called. The roster no longer renders an internal `user_{id}` string as a username. With the data pre-built, the layout follows: Active Students first, the join code folded into a collapsible Unclaimed seats card (expanded while seats are unclaimed, collapsed once all are claimed), the redundant outer "Class Roster" card removed, and the active class shown as the pane's heading instead of muted body text.
- **Unclaimed seats no longer take part in the economy or appear on teacher surfaces (2026-09-17)** — An unclaimed seat keeps its balance and history but is not an economic participant, so it is excluded from every economic run and every teacher-facing figure, list and log. Fixed: the teacher dashboard counted unclaimed seats and included a balance retained through unclaim (16 students and $20 of economy value where Banking and Payroll showed 3 and $0.00); payroll-cycle settlement paid unclaimed seats, because attendance rows survive unclaim and were treated as proof of eligibility; the savings-interest sweep had neither a claim nor a role filter, so it paid unclaimed seats and teacher seats every month; the class-wide bonus paid unclaimed seats, and had been raising before reaching the ledger since it wrapped a one-element list in `sa.select(...)`; manual credit, tap-in, tap-out, bulk hall-pass entitlements and store visibility accepted unclaimed seats; the Banking transaction log, payroll history, rent counts and waiver list (which also counted the teacher's own seat as a student), dashboard attendance log, attendance-history API and the balances export displayed them. Payroll and attendance are now enforced fail-closed at their domain commands. Retained rows are never deleted or swept — a balance stays the seat's own for re-claim. DOM-IDEN-002 v2.7 records the rule.
- **Unclaim button meets text contrast (2026-09-17)** — `.btn-outline-warning` used the raw `--warning` gold, 2.2:1 on white. It now uses `--accent-text-on-light` (6.7:1 on white, 5.5:1 on the grey row), with dark text on the gold fill on hover, focus and active (7.9:1). A token-level test computes the WCAG ratios from `tokens.css` and `style.css`.
- **Teacher sign-out no longer shows a system admin message (2026-09-17)** — Pages poll `/admin/onboarding/status` in the background; a poll that landed after sign-out flashed "System admin session is invalid" into the next page. Teacher session failures now say "Your session has ended" or "Your session expired", and background requests (non-navigation fetches, JSON and XHR) get a 401 JSON response without a flashed message.
- **Unclaim assigns claim codes like import (2026-09-17)** — Unclaiming a seat whose claim names match another unclaimed seat no longer asks the teacher for a distinguishing code or rejects the change. The system assigns distinct codes to every unclaimed seat with those names, shown in the Unclaimed Seats list. Import and unclaim share one code-assignment path. The roster test CSVs carry a UTF-8 byte-order mark so spreadsheets keep accented names intact.
- **Student guide for teacher recovery matches the current flow (2026-09-17)** — The guide now explains that students are randomly selected, that codes last 30 minutes and a new code replaces the old one, that codes go to the teacher only in person while the teacher is with the class, and uses the current button labels. Removes the unused `remove_student_from_teacher_scope` identity FEAT wrapper; routes call the deletion utility inside their own FEAT.
- **Student-assisted teacher recovery requires at least 3 claimed students per class (2026-09-17)** — Recovery randomly picks 2 recipients per class; with fewer than 3 eligible students that pick is predictable, so the proof and recipient selection now fail closed below 3 instead of selecting the only student. The recovery page, recovery setup page, Student Management roster (while the active class is below 3), teacher guide and public pages state the requirement and why (DOM-IDEN-003 §IX, FEAT-IDEN-103).
- **Teacher recovery requires more student usernames for teachers with few classes (2026-09-17)** — Starting recovery now requires 6 student usernames for a single class, 3 per class for two classes, 2 per class for three, and 1 per class for four or more, or every claimed student in a smaller class. A student username can back only one pair. The whole proof set is checked in one request that creates the attempt only on success; resolved students are held in memory and never stored, and uniqueness is checked on the resolved account rather than the typed text. The per-class proof endpoint is removed. Random recipient selection stays independent in each class and never considers a student's seats in other classes. This keeps a single student from starting a recovery attempt that would lock their teacher out of recovery for five days (DOM-IDEN-003 §IX v2.8, FEAT-IDEN-103 v1.4).
- **Help text and public pages describe current claim and reset behavior (2026-09-17)** — Claim codes are described as system-generated, name edits as display-only, and student login reset as a ten-minute recovery code rather than a new claim.
- **Roster names link to student detail, which shows teacher notes (2026-09-16)** — Student names on the payroll Manual Payments picker and the Rent roster link to the student detail page, so a teacher can tell students with the same name apart before acting. Payroll opens detail in a new tab to keep the half-filled form. The detail page now displays teacher notes outside the edit form.
- **Claim lifecycle and external hall-pass verification (2026-09-15)** — Clear temporary name hashes, roster fingerprints, and distinguishing codes when a student claims a seat. Recovery and name edits do not recreate them. Hall-pass verification uses capability-authorized, class-scoped profile names and same-day records. Additive roster imports always create new seats. The system assigns distinct claim codes to every unclaimed seat that shares a name, including existing unclaimed namesakes, and the Unclaimed Seats list shows them. Removing a student deletes only that seat's pending actions, not the whole class's. A data migration clears older retained claim material. Targeted identity, recovery, deletion, and hall-pass tests passed, including rendered form accessibility checks.

### Documentation

- **Links point at the repository's current name (2026-09-20)** — `classroom-economy` is the repository's retired name and `classroom-token-hub` is current, and thirty links across twelve files still used the old one: the public marketing pages, the in-app documentation templates, the legal user guides, a runner setup script, and `docs-site/docusaurus.config.js`, whose `repoUrl` and `projectName` drive the "Edit this page" link on every page of the documentation site. GitHub redirects a renamed repository, so all of them resolved and nothing looked broken — the redirect is a courtesy, not a guarantee, and it lapses the moment the old name is taken by another repository. `README.md` was wrong in a way the redirect could not hide: it told a new contributor to `cd classroom-economy` after cloning, which produces a directory of the other name. **The retired name is deliberately left in place where it is not a repository reference.** It is the live systemd unit and the deployment directory on the production host — `.github/workflows/release-v2.yml` restarts `classroom-economy` and works in `~/classroom-economy`, and `CONTRIBUTING.md` documents that same path — so rewriting the prose would have made it wrong and rewriting the workflow would have broken the deploy. It is also the issuer label in a TOTP `otpauth://` URI, where a change would invalidate every enrolled authenticator, and it appears as the ordinary English phrase "classroom-economy boundary". `tests/test_repository_url_is_current.py` matches the repository-URL form only, so those are outside the rule by construction rather than by exception, and `docs/archive/` is exempt because a link that was correct when archived is history. Its mutation proofs cover the five link shapes that actually occurred and eight near misses, including a repository whose name merely starts with the retired one and the same path under a different owner.

- **The public documentation site stops publishing working state (2026-09-20)** — the site builds from `../docs` with an *exclude* list, which fails open: a file dropped into a directory is published unless someone remembers to exclude it. Nobody had decided to publish an operator's filled deployment worksheet, a 1,267-line live-deployment record, or raw pytest evidence from one run; they were published because they were markdown files in a directory that was. `docs/ops/**` is now excluded in full — a deployment record describes one operation of one host, not the software, and nothing in the public tree linked to it — and its autogenerated sidebar category is removed, since an empty `autogenerated` category fails the build. `docs/TRACKING/` keeps only the two documents durable enough for an outside reader, `DOCS_PLATFORM_ROADMAP.md` and `DOMAIN_IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN_TEMPLATE.md`; the other fourteen are dated snapshots of in-progress decisions. `README_DOMAIN_TRACKING.md` is excluded with them despite being an index rather than a snapshot: it is a table of contents *for* the excluded documents, and publishing it alone produced a page whose every entry 404s — which is how the build surfaced it. `tests/test_docs_site_publishes_no_working_state.py` inverts the default so this cannot drift back: anything under `ops/` or `TRACKING/` must be matched by the exclude list or appear in an explicit `PUBLISHABLE` allowlist, so a new document defaults to not published and the build fails until someone chooses. It ships with mutation proofs over a pure matcher, including the case that `**` must span path separators and that a quoted path inside a comment is not a live pattern. Two further inversions surfaced while doing it. `SOP-DOC-001`, a normative index, linked into `docs/TRACKING/` for reachability; those citations are now repository paths rather than links, because a normative document should not depend on a descriptive one being published. And `tests/test_normative_docs_are_not_worksheets.py` now also covers the case that produced the original worksheet.

- **A normative document cannot be used as a worksheet (2026-09-20)** — `tests/test_normative_docs_are_not_worksheets.py` fails on any ticked task-list item under the five normative roots. The failure it guards against has already happened: `SOP-DEP-001` is prose with no checkboxes, so an operator who wanted something tickable forked its sections into `docs/TRACKING/`, filled the copy in during a real deployment, and committed it — a parallel copy of a governing procedure carrying one run's host state, at a tier `CLAUDE.md` marks descriptive. Untracking that file removed the instance; this removes the class. `docs/TRACKING/`, `docs/MAP/` and `docs/ops/` are deliberately outside the rule, because a tracking document or a deployment record is *supposed* to carry ticked boxes — that is what those tiers are for. Fenced code is skipped so a document may still show the filled markup as an example, and the detector resumes after the fence closes. Per `SOP-TEST-003` §IX.A the detection is a pure function over source text with a companion mutation proof: five synthetic violations spelled the way a real one would be, and six near-miss negatives covering empty boxes, prose mentioning the markup, both fence styles, and bullets that are not task items. Also closes a hole in the previous entry's fix: `.gitignore` keeps a filled worksheet out of the *repository*, but the documentation site builds from the working tree, so a site built on the operator's own machine would have published the file regardless. The Docusaurus config now excludes the worksheet pattern, which is the layer that actually governs what reaches the public site.

- **The working deployment checklist is untracked, and not replaced by a template (2026-09-20)** — `docs/TRACKING/PRODUCTION_FIRST_TEST_DEPLOYMENT_CHECKLIST_2026-09.md` carried live host state under a `docs/TRACKING/` path, which `CLAUDE.md` marks descriptive rather than authoritative, while `SOP-DEP-001_Live_Test_Runbook.md` — namespaced, normative, and tracked — already covered every one of its sections as a superset: its seven numbered steps map onto §VI, §VII, §VIII, §IX, §X, §XI and §XIV, and its "Current state" onto §V. It was a parallel copy of a governing procedure, held at a lower authority tier, which is the same failure this month's design work spent four commits undoing on the stylesheets. It is now untracked and ignored; the operator's filled copy stays local. **No blank template replaces it** — publishing one would recreate the duplication in a new file, and a second document that must be kept in step with the first is a fork with a delay. `SOP-DEP-001` is the checklist, and an operator wanting a worksheet copies it. Its version-2.0 provenance note cited the untracked file as "the source of current host truth", a normative document depending on a descriptive one; that citation now points at §V. The checklist contained no secret values — its environment section is a list of variable names carrying an explicit instruction not to commit or print values — but it did name the host, its access path, and the service and database names. Those same facts remain in §V of `SOP-DEP-001` and in `SOP-DEP-002`, both public and both tracked, so untracking the checklist narrows the disclosure without ending it; that is recorded here rather than implied.

- **`PRN-PHL-001` separates what is true from how it got true (2026-09-20)** — the philosophy document interleaved durable principles with a milestone narrative, and a reader had no way to tell one from the other. Its central architectural claim was the inverse of the system: v1.2 stated that the join code was "the absolute source of truth" and that this had "reshaped the entire data model", while `class_id` has been canonical since the v2 identity work and `CanonicalContext.__getattr__` *raises* on `join_code`. The sentence was accurate when written, which is the point — it was never wrong, only old, and nothing in the document's shape could say so. v2.0 splits it into **Part I, what is true now** (present tense, every architectural claim checkable against the code and the governing contract) and **Part II, how it got here** (past tense, each milestone marked Current, Superseded or Evolved, a superseded one naming its replacement). Three factual corrections come with the split: the architecture file-tree omitted `feats/` and `services/` — the mutation and domain layers, 27 and 48 modules — so the diagram described a v1 codebase; blueprint isolation was described as mirroring classroom section isolation, which conflates role-scoped blueprints with the `class_id` scoping that actually enforces tenancy, and the two fail in opposite ways (a blueprint boundary cannot leak, a missing `class_id` filter is a one-line omission inside a correct-looking query); and the join-code milestone is rewritten as superseded, keeping the reasoning that survived — class membership must be deterministic, isolated and recoverable — while naming the key that now carries it. The v1 → v2 identity migration is added as a milestone, having been the largest architectural event in the project and absent from the document. Visual identity history is pointed at `REF-DES-001` rather than restated, because a summary of a history is how a history goes stale. An Amendment section now requires that a superseded decision move to Part II rather than be deleted: removing a reversal makes the project's progression look inevitable, which it was not.

- **Review corrections to the design gate (2026-09-20)** — two defects in the gate added above, both of which made it report clean where it should not have. The R8 scrim exemption was evaluated against the whole declaration rather than against each literal, so a scrim sitting next to a brand colour exempted the brand colour with it: `box-shadow: 0 0 0 1px rgba(0,0,0,.1), 0 0 0 3px rgba(212,168,87,.4)` passed, which is the shape of a real focus ring. `color_literals()` now returns the complete `rgb`/`rgba`/`hsl`/`hsla` call rather than a bare `rgba)`, so the exemption can be judged per literal. And `scan_mirrors()` skipped a pair when either file was absent, so deleting or renaming either side switched R10 off in silence — this rule's own failure mode applied to itself; a missing side is now a finding. Both carry mutation proofs, and reverting either defect turns three of them red. One accessibility defect is corrected with them: `.sysadmin-nav-signout` set its label with `--sysadmin-signout-accent` composited to 85%, which was **2.91:1** on the sidebar. The dilution was the defect — diluting a text colour to suggest a resting state is what §IX already forbids for link hovers — so the label takes the token at full opacity and the token is pinned at `#ff8080`, 5.75:1, with the existing `#ff6666` hover at 4.88:1. The 40% border composite is chrome rather than text and is unchanged. Also: `--radius-xs` and `--font-data` were restated as literals five times in `status-overrides.css` and now reference their tokens, which required adding `--radius-xs` to the shared public token block — R9 caught that omission on the first run after the edit. The `--radius-xl` comment in `tokens.css` still described auth cards, superseded by §IX.

- **The design gate covers all four brand surfaces, and finds the app was not clean (2026-09-20)** — `scripts/lint_design_tokens.py` scanned `templates/` only, so three of the four surfaces §XIII names were gated by nothing. The application was the one gated surface and also the one that had not drifted, which is not a coincidence. Three rules are added over the token layers and stylesheets of all four: **R8**, no colour literal outside a declaration that *defines* a token — a property test rather than a selector test, since the role themes are token blocks mounted on `body.student-shell` and `body.sysadmin-shell`, not on `:root`; **R9**, no `var(--token)` the surface's own token layer does not define, since such a declaration is invalid and falls back in silence; and **R10**, a stylesheet declared a copy of another is byte-identical to it. An achromatic value inside a colour function — `color-mix(…, black)`, `rgba(0,0,0,.08)` — is a shading operation and is admitted; standing alone as a whole value it is not, because `color: white` is `--text-inverse` spelled wrong. Framework namespaces (`--bs-`, `--ifm-`) are exempt per surface. Turning the rules on reported that `static/css/style.css` was never clean, only unchecked: nine declarations restated `--secondary` as `rgba(212, 168, 87, α)` on the sysadmin sidebar rather than composing from the token, four more restated `--sysadmin-sidebar-text` as a `var()` fallback, a `--sysadmin-sidebar-bg` fallback restated `#2C2C2C`, and thirty-five bare `white` / `#ffffff` / `#000` values stood where `--text-inverse`, `--surface` and `--neutral-900` belong. All are composed from tokens now, and `--sysadmin-signout-accent` is named rather than inlined per §VI.5. R9 found the defect the rule exists for: `style.css` referenced `var(--secondary-color)` on seven lines, which is a **public-site** token name that nothing in `static/css/tokens.css` defines, so seven declarations had been invalid and falling back with no symptom. One more survivor of the 2026-09-19 hex sweep surfaced in `status-overrides.css`: the retired v1 gold `#d3af37`, in `rgba(211,175,55,.35)` form, which a search for hex literals could not see. Per `SOP-TEST-003` §IX.A each new rule ships with a mutation proof — a synthetic near-miss the test asserts is reported, spelled the way a real regression would spell it. Blinding the scanner in the three plausible ways (admitting achromatics everywhere, accepting any unknown token, comparing mirror sizes instead of bytes) turns six of them red.

- **`--warning-hover` converges, and the auth card is documented square (2026-09-20)** — the token differed three ways (`#a87912` teacher, `#b88f22` student and sysadmin) while `--warning` was the same gold in all three, with nothing recorded to justify the split; two themes agreeing is the shape of a copy-paste rather than a decision. All three take `#a87912`, commented like `--secondary-hover` above it. Separately, §VI.1 assigned `--radius-xl` to "auth cards and feature panels" and `.auth-container` applies no `border-radius` at all, so the spec documented a rounded card that fourteen pages have never rendered. The square card is the intended form — it is coherent with the flat, border-over-shadow treatment in §V.1.3 — so the specification moves to the implementation rather than the reverse, and §IX records why. SPEC-DES-001 is at v2.2; §XIII is rewritten, since the public brand split it described as live was reconciled on 2026-09-19, and the requirement it now carries is the byte-identity of the status service's copy plus the subordination of `status-overrides.css` to this document.

- **The status service stops carrying its own brand (2026-09-20)** — `status_service/` held four stylesheets, two of them undeclared forks, and was the only one of the project's four surfaces gated by nothing. `static/theme.css` still defined `--primary: #236960` and `--secondary: #ac8255` — the v1 values SPEC-DES-001 §VI.4 retired — and `static/cth-public.css` was an 876-line copy of `github-pages/style.css` frozen immediately before the 2026-09-19 reconciliation above, so the public site's brand fix never reached the status page. The two diverged on 34 lines, including `.toc a:hover`, which the public copy had already moved to `--accent-text-on-light` and the fork still rendered at `#ac8255`: the same selector was accessible on one host and not on the other. `cth-public.css` is now byte-identical to `github-pages/style.css`, which makes that class of split visible as a diff rather than invisible as a copy. `static/status-overrides.css` carried 32 colour literals across 12 hues, and a single service state rendered in four different ambers depending on which of five components drew it — `#ffbd00` as a banner fill, `#b06f2c` as a card border, `#b45e19` and `#8a531e` as pill text. Every one now resolves to a foundation token: the amber family to `--warning` with `--alert-warning-text` on it, monitoring from `#4f86d9` to `--info`, available from `#43ad42` to `--success`, and the greys to the neutral ramp. Net new hues: zero. Because §XI.2 forbids carrying a state in colour alone, the status dots now differ in shape as well as fill — investigating square, identified square with an inset ring, monitoring a hollow circle — which survives a greyscale screenshot and a colour-blind reader. The status quartet and the three alert text tokens move into the shared public token block, since the status service consumes that stylesheet and had been composing `var(--font-data)` against a file that never defined it, silently rendering capability ids in Inter rather than IBM Plex Mono. Four files are deleted as a dead chain: `templates/base.html` and `templates/base_public.html` are extended by nothing — `app.py` renders only `public_status.html` and `operator_notices.html`, both of which extend `base_public_v2.html` — and `static/status.css` existed only to `@import` `theme.css`, which is how the retired palette stayed reachable while rendering for no one. `tests/test_status_projection.py` fetches `/` and reads the rendered page, so the deletion is covered by the existing suite.

- **`--accent` is pinned to `--secondary` in every role theme (2026-09-20)** — the teacher and student themes aliased it; the sysadmin theme stated `#FDB034`. One alias, three values, which is the §X anti-pattern inside the token file the rule is written for: any shared component tinted by `--accent` changed hue for operators alone, with nothing to signal it. The brighter gold is still available as `--sysadmin-accent-bright`, two lines above, which is what it was wanted for. `docs-site/src/css/tokens.css` gains the fourteen tokens it had fallen behind on, the three `--alert-*-text` values first: the guide site renders admonitions, and without them an admonition reaches for a status token, which is a fill and not a text colour. The underlying cause is unaddressed and remains the largest item — the base layer is transcribed by hand into four consumers, so all four drift independently, and generating it from one source would make most of this class of defect impossible rather than fixed.

- **Visual identity history (2026-09-20)** — Add `REF-DES-001` as an informative history from April 2025 through the September 20 repository snapshot, covering role-color predecessors and literal values, logos and wordmarks, typography, layout reversals, and public-site divergence. Commit citations distinguish implementation evidence and recorded reasons from inference and unknowns, including the direct `#2F4F7F` Dark Slate Blue → Steward Blue lineage. Register the reference in `SOP-DOC-001` v3.6.

- **The public site carries the canonical brand values (2026-09-19)** — `github-pages/style.css` defined `--primary-color: #236960`, `--primary-dark: #1a4742`, `--secondary-color: #ac8255`, `--accent-color: #c49564`, `--secondary: #d3af37` and `--secondary-hover: #c49f2e`. None matched SPEC-DES-001 §VI.4, and §XIII named the split as tracked work with the published site as the artifact that was wrong. With the developer docs site now living at `/docs/` on the same host, a reader crossing from the marketing footer moved between two different greens. The four legacy alias names are kept — the stylesheet consumes them throughout — but now reference the canonical tokens rather than restating a literal, per §X: an alias holding its own hex stops following the token it shadows, which is how the split survived the last correction. `--secondary` and `--secondary-hover` take the canonical gold pair. One contrast defect surfaced and is fixed rather than carried forward: `.toc a:hover` set gold text on the `--neutral-100` table-of-contents card, which was 3.16:1 at the old `#ac8255` and would have become 2.02:1 at the canonical gold; it now uses `--accent-text-on-light` (`#735816`, 6.13:1 on that surface), the token §VI.6 exists for. `--accent-text-on-light` is added to the public token set for that purpose. A correction to the entry above: the gold-on-Advisor-Green heading was recorded there as 4.76:1; it is **4.34:1**, which clears the 3:1 large-text threshold that governs it — the heading is 30px at weight 600 — but not the 4.5:1 normal-text one, so gold must not set body text on that band. The docs-site comment stating the wrong figure is corrected. `tests/dom/docs/test_docs_site_brand_conformance.py` now checks the public site's tokens against `static/css/tokens.css` as well, and asserts the four aliases resolve through `var()`; run against the pre-fix stylesheet it reports four failures naming `#d3af37` and `#236960`.

- **The FEAT registry and the FEAT contracts are audited against each other (2026-09-19)** — `app/feats/base.py` registers 46 FEAT ids; `docs/FEATURE-EXECUTION/` holds 40 contracts; they overlap on 35. Neither side notices: `requires_feat_context` accepts any registered id whether or not a contract exists, and a contract with no registry entry breaks nothing at runtime, so the drift is invisible except by comparing the two lists. `docs/TRACKING/FEAT_REGISTRY_RECONCILIATION_2026-09-19.md` records every mismatch with its evidence and a proposed disposition, and decides none of them — which id owns which workflow is a domain-ownership ruling.
