The one item with a correctness consequence rather than a documentation one: `FEAT-OBL-001` (registry, "Rent Payment") and `FEAT-OBLI-001` (contract, "Assess Obligation") differ by one letter, and `app/feats/assess_obligation_feat.py` implements the second while executing under the first, so assessment events are audited as rent payments and the file's own comment cites a §V of a document that does not exist. Six further registered ids execute in production with no contract — `FEAT-SETTINGS-001`, `FEAT-OBL-001`, `FEAT-OBL-005`, `FEAT-ADMN-001` (whose "Logistics" domain appears in no `DOM-*` document), `FEAT-IDEN-005`, and `FEAT-LED-003` (HIGH blast radius, no production caller). Two are registered without executing (`FEAT-LED-004`, recorded by `MAP-UI-001` as rewired away on 2026-07-21; `FEAT-ITR-001`, awaiting its executor), and three are self-labelled `[RETIRED → …]` tombstones. On the other side, the passkey contracts `FEAT-IDEN-102` and `FEAT-IDEN-107` describe code that exists but runs under no FEAT id.

- **SOP-DOC-001 indexes the whole corpus again (2026-09-19)** — The canonical documentation index listed 85 of the 131 numbered documents while §I described itself as "the canonical master index of all formal documentation". The 46 absences were not obscure: nearly every `FEAT-*` contract, `SOP-DB-001` (migrations), `SOP-DEP-001`/`002` (deployment and live-test runbooks), `SOP-SEC-001`, `SPEC-DES-001` (the design system), `SPEC-UI-001` and `INV-ARC-022`. Every link it did carry resolved, which is why the gap survived a link audit in v3.2 and the reconciliation in v3.3 — an index fails by silence, and silence leaves no broken link to find. v3.4 lists all 131 (132 files, counting the `DOM-POL-001A` appendix), each entry carrying its document title, ordered by reference number within namespace sections. §V is renamed from "V2 Restructured Specifications" to "Registered Documents": the old title framed the list as the subset that had been ported, which is what licensed it to stay partial, and there is no unported remainder left to distinguish it from. The two unnumbered `docs/TRACKING/` records §V cited are kept in their own subsection, marked descriptive rather than registered, per §II. `tests/dom/docs/test_documentation_index_complete.py` now fails when a numbered document exists with no entry, when an entry names a document that does not exist, or when any link resolves to nothing; its mutation proof adds a FEAT contract to a synthetic tree and asserts the detector reports it, and the completeness test was watched failing against v3.3 (47 missing) and against v3.4 with one entry deleted.

- **The docs site navigates to entry points, not to a blog (2026-09-19)** — The navbar's second item was the Docusaurus blog. It now carries **All documents**, **Glossary**, and a **Reference** menu, and the landing page adds a "Go straight there" list: the glossary, the HTTP interface reference, how to write a migration, how to write a test, the template-to-FEAT wiring map, self-hosting, and the notes. Each target was checked for currency before being linked, and two failed. `SOP-DOC-001` — the canonical index — had intact links but listed 85 of the 131 numbered documents in the tree (reconciled to all 131 in the entry above); alongside it, `/documents` is generated from the docs plugin's own routing data and lists all 153 published pages, naming SOP-DOC-001 as the index of record and itself as navigation only. `MAP-CORE-001` (v1.0, 2026-04-23) predates the numbered FEAT registry and names orchestrators that were never created — `FEAT-IDEN-LOGIN`, `FEAT-CLASS-INIT`, `FEAT-CLASS-POLICY` — so it stays published but is not offered as an entry point. Two further gaps were found and left for their owners: `MAP-UI-001` attributes four store and attendance surfaces to `FEAT-SETTINGS-001`, which is registered in `app/feats/base.py` and enforced in `app/feats/attendance.py` but has no contract document under `docs/FEATURE-EXECUTION/`; and `REF-TERM-001` (v1.0, 2026-06-15) is architecturally current — it treats `StudentBlock`, `ClassMembership` and `balance_cache` as retired — but predates the Interpretation domain and the idempotency-reservation vocabulary.

- **The docs site renders in the project's visual identity (2026-09-19)** — `docs-site/` shipped stock Docusaurus: Infima blue, Google-hosted Inter, 12px radii, drop shadows on resting cards, and a dark theme with a palette invented for it. SPEC-DES-001 §XIII names `docs-site/` a brand surface that must not contradict the canonical values in §VI.4, and none of that conformed. The site now carries the Teacher (Advisor Green) identity — a documentation reader holds no role, so it takes the default one. `docs-site/src/css/tokens.css` transcribes the base layer plus that theme from `static/css/tokens.css` and is the only place in the workspace a design value appears as a literal; `custom.css` maps Infima's variables onto those tokens and states no value of its own, which a test now asserts by scanning it for raw colour. Radii follow §VI.1 (4px cards, 6px buttons) instead of a uniform 12px, resting surfaces are flat with elevation reserved for overlay (§V.1.3), motion uses the `--duration-*` scale under a `prefers-reduced-motion` override, and focus-visible styling is stated once for the site rather than left per-component (§XI.3). Typography is the product's: Inter for body, Atkinson Hyperlegible Next for headings, IBM Plex Mono for code and identifiers, served from the same self-hosted woff2 files the application ships — the Google Fonts import is gone. The landing page leads with the canonical brand mark: the three-row `CLASSROOM` / `TOKEN` / `HUB` wordmark with its Material Symbols and gold rule on a `--primary` band, mirroring `templates/macros/docs_hero.html`, because §IX requires the brand to be text and prohibits a logo file. Material Symbols is subset to those three glyphs — 1.6KB against the 3.9MB full face, small enough that the bundler inlines it. The colour-mode switch is disabled: the design system defines one identity per role and all three are light, so a dark theme could only be built from values §VI.4 does not define. The same test also asserts that every project token the site's stylesheets reference is defined, which catches a failure with no visual symptom to review against: CSS drops a whole declaration when a `var()` inside a shorthand resolves to nothing, so `border: var(--space-hairline) solid var(--border-color)` against an undefined `--space-hairline` renders no border at all and falls back to `currentColor`. `tests/dom/docs/test_docs_site_brand_conformance.py` compares the site's tokens against the application's — reading only the `:root` blocks, since the token layer states each role-dependent token three times and a flat read would compare against Guardian Gray — and carries a mutation proof using `#d3af37`, the retired v1 gold. The separate `github-pages/` brand split §XIII records (`--primary-color: #236960`, `--secondary: #d3af37`) is untouched and still open.

- **The developer documentation site publishes the documentation tree (2026-09-19)** — `docs-site/` held four placeholder technical pages and a copy of the user guides, and nothing deployed it. It now builds directly out of `../docs` and publishes the developer-facing tree — `INVARIANT/`, `DOMAIN/`, `FEATURE-EXECUTION/`, `SPEC/`, `STANDARD_OPERATING_PROCEDURES/`, `MAP/`, `REFERENCE/`, `PRINCIPLES/`, `ops/`, `self-hosting/`, `TRACKING/` — with the sidebar ordered by the authority chain and its labels held in `sidebars.js` so no generator metadata lands in the normative tree. Nothing is copied into the workspace, so a document cannot have a second rendered version that drifts from the first. `docs/user-guides`, `docs/archive` and `docs/assets` are excluded: the application owns the help centre, and that boundary now has the same shape in `app/utils/helpers.py`, `app/routes/docs.py`, and the docs plugin's exclude list. `.github/workflows/github-pages.yml` replaces the transition-site workflow and assembles one Pages artifact — the marketing pages at `/` and this site at `/docs/` — deploying on `main` and building on matching pull requests; the four marketing pages link to it from their footers. `tests/dom/docs/test_public_pages_link_to_developer_docs.py` states that as a rule over every HTML file in `github-pages/` rather than over the four that exist today: the landing branch replaces `index.html` and adds `learnmore.html`, neither of which carries the link, so the guard is what tells whoever merges it that the link has to come along. The assembled artifact was served through a resolver that mimics GitHub Pages' extension-stripping (`/foo` → `foo.html`) and walked from each public page into the docs site — every request 200, no console errors. Edit links use the function form of `editUrl`, since the plugin reads from `../docs` and a string base has that relative path appended verbatim, producing `/edit/main/docs/../docs/<file>`. The offline link check gains `root_dir = "."`: the public pages link to `/docs/` root-relatively because that is where the docs site sits in the Pages artifact, and a root-relative link cannot be resolved against a repository that has no root — lychee errors while building the URL, before any `exclude` pattern is consulted. The build runs with `onBrokenLinks`, `onBrokenAnchors` and `onBrokenMarkdownLinks` set to throw, so renaming a document under `docs/` without fixing its inbound links fails the build rather than shipping a dead link. `route-map.json` is now exceptions only: a developer-facing path requested at `app.classroomtokenhub.com/docs/<path>` forwards to the same path on the docs site instead of collapsing to its root, which had been silently dropping the reader's deep link, and the map carries only documents whose path changed on the way over. Site dependencies were refreshed (React 19.3.0; an `image-size` override clears GHSA-w3rx-r6r6-pgpr), and the brand palette applies for the first time: Infima states its own colors at `:root:not(#\#):not(#\#)`, two id-worth of specificity, so the site had been rendering in stock Docusaurus blue regardless of `custom.css`. `tests/dom/docs/test_docs_platform_split.py` gains deep-link, route-map and boundary coverage, including a mutation proof that the exclude-list detector fails a config that stops excluding the help centre.

- **Structural guards must prove they catch something (2026-09-18)** — SOP-TEST-003 v1.2 adds §IX.A and a sixth standard test shape. A structural guard — a scan of source, templates or migrations, a decorator or registry check — is normally added to a codebase that already complies, so it passes from the moment it is written and §IX.2 ("the test fails for the prior broken behavior *when that behavior is still present*") is satisfied vacuously. Nothing then distinguishes "no violation exists" from "the detector stopped detecting", and the green check gets read as proof the violation is impossible. Each guard now ships with a mutation proof: the detector is a pure function over source text or a parsed tree, the repo-wide test asserts no offender exists, and a companion test feeds it a synthetic violation and asserts it is reported — with the mutation written as a near miss rather than a caricature, lawful inputs asserted quiet, and, where the detector locates targets by a renameable form, an assertion that it still finds them in the real source. The four guards added on this branch were retrofitted: the `actor_public_id` foreign-key scan, the claim-material derivation check, the destruction-FEAT decorator check, and the Bootstrap/token template scans. The rationale is recorded from two failures in one session — a guard matching likely variable names that let an injected `_probe_profile.first_name` through, and two migration tests asserting a cascade the governing contract forbade.


- **FEAT-IDEN-006 and FEAT-IDEN-007 no longer designate a provisioning FEAT as the executor of principal destruction (2026-09-17)** — Both contracts authorized the attribution defect fixed in the roster deletion path. FEAT-IDEN-006 §Roster removal had this FEAT "compose Identity removal or class/account destruction as appropriate", and FEAT-IDEN-007 §Automatic and roster-terminal entry points said the account-destruction command is "composed by the final-student roster deletion FEAT" — naming FEAT-IDEN-006, blast radius MED, as the executing FEAT for terminal destruction of a teacher principal. FEAT-IDEN-006 now owns roster removal only and states that the route dispatches to the owning FEAT before it opens; FEAT-IDEN-007 now claims roster-terminal account destruction on the same terms its §Composition already used for `POST /admin/join-code/delete`, which brings the roster path in line with the scheduled retention entry that already executed under FEAT-IDEN-007 with the same fail-closed re-evaluation under lock. Nesting stays forbidden and atomicity is unchanged: the three roster outcomes are mutually exclusive, so selecting the FEAT before it opens still yields one envelope per request. Class destruction moved to its own FEAT in the same PR (see below).
- **SOP-DB-001 v1.3 names a second, narrower exception to Golden Rule 3 (2026-09-17)** — Two merged migrations were edited on this branch to guard operations on columns that no longer exist: `3a69db4907b4` (`announcements.user_id`) and `8f1a2c3d4b5e` (`policy_transitions.created_by`), both lost when authorship moved to `created_by_seat_id`. Those edits do **not** qualify under §V.A, whose condition 1 excludes "a replay that is merely untidy, that errors, or that adds drift without destroying, rewriting, or invalidating protected state" — nothing is destroyed here, the migration simply raises. They are also load-bearing: without them a fresh `flask db upgrade` fails at `3a69db4907b4`, so a new production database would not boot. New §V.B defines a Bootstrap-Replay Correction for exactly this case, with seven conditions, an explicit prohibition list, and a register recording why each of the two qualifies. It permits only the guard that declines an operation when `0001_bootstrap` has left its target column absent; it may not change what the migration does when the column is present, may not alter the migration's intended end state, and may not retarget the operation onto the replacement column. Both migrations now carry a comment naming the section and the absent column. The root cause is recorded as post-launch architectural debt in `docs/TRACKING/PRODUCTION_READINESS_2026-09.md` §VI: `0001_bootstrap` materializes *today's* ORM metadata rather than a frozen baseline, so removing a model can retroactively break an unrelated historical revision while the revision graph looks untouched. The bootstrap already remedies this for whole tables (`_create_retired_baseline_tables`) and has no equivalent for columns. The remedy is a frozen baseline snapshot, and since the bootstrap is itself a merged migration that is a baseline replacement rather than an edit under §V.B. Verified: one head (`d9e1f3a5b7c9`), migration lint clean against the frozen baseline (0 errors), and the full chain replays on a fresh schema in every test-session rebuild. No downgrade evidence is claimed and none is required: `c7a7b8c9d0e1` refuses downgrade by design and sits later in the chain, so neither corrected revision is reachable by a downgrade from head. §V.B condition 7 says so explicitly rather than demanding a rehearsal this chain cannot produce. The register also records one thing left uncorrected on purpose — `3a69db4907b4`'s `downgrade()` ends with an unguarded `alter_column` on the same absent column, which is the identical defect in reverse but is unreachable, and guarding it would be tidying a merged migration.
- **Public pages: one frame each, and three disclosure claims corrected against the invariants (2026-09-17)** — The privacy page mixed voices, with "you" addressing the student in one paragraph and the teacher in the next; it now uses a single descriptive frame that names the actor. The district page already had one frame and needed only the corrections below. Terms keeps its second person, where "you agree" is the acceptance mechanism, but §3 and §4 are neutral and the audience-specific blocks stay labelled. Three claims were wrong, each overstating what CTH discloses: "Nicknames do not make linked classroom records anonymous" (a roster built entirely from nicknames leaves no real-name identifier in the platform at all); "decrypted for authorized application views" (roster notes are visible to the provisioning teacher alone, display names only in that teacher's roster and the student's own interface — support escalation carries neither, in any branch of `support_disclosure.py`); and hall-pass verification "can show that student's display name" (INV-ARC-019: the response is limited to "the supplied name's presence in that class and same-day hall-pass metadata" — the staff member supplies both the class and the name, so a match confirms what they already hold and adds only the pass facts). Terms also said an account may be "suspended", which contradicted its own §6 and the codebase: there is no suspended state, an account exists or has been deleted with its records.
- **Design-system tokens gained the sub-base steps a compact component needs (2026-09-17)** — The Getting Started widget carried 13 literal values in its `<style>` block, failing SPEC-DES-001 R6; the docs viewer carried one more. Five had exact token equivalents and were swapped without any rendered change (`18px`→`--icon-sm`, `20px`→`--icon-md`, `16px`→`--text-base`, `1.4`→`--leading-snug`, `1.25rem`→`--text-xl`). The remaining values had no token at all, so three were added rather than rounding the widget to the nearest step: `--space-hairline` (1px) and `--space-half` (2px) for controls that must stay compact inside a dense component, `--leading-body` (1.5) for body copy in a narrow column, and `--alpha-heavy` (0.85) for an element deliberately one step back from its neighbours. The two resting opacities (0.80 and 0.85) now share that one token. Also fixed: `test_obligation_view_models` hand-assembled a `Seat` with `user_id` set and no `claimed_at` — a state DOM-IDEN-002 §Student Seat State forbids — which the class rent summary correctly ignored once it scoped to claimed student seats; the fixture now creates a lawfully claimed seat.
- **MAP-UI-001 re-verified; most outstanding rewire work was already done (2026-09-17)** — The wiring map listed a large backlog of `NEEDS_REWIRE` capability rows, and its own summary counts (`NEEDS_REWIRE` 15+, `VERIFY_ONLY` 8+) exceeded the number of rows the section holds. Each row was checked against the code: 7 are `REWIRED` (class creation through FEAT-CLASS-001, feature enable/disable through FEAT-CLASS-004, policy transition through FEAT-CLASS-005, class deletion through FEAT-CLASS-001 escalating to FEAT-IDEN-007, the student rent surface through the obligation view model, and the roster through its new view model), 3 are `OBSOLETE` because the route or template was deleted or renamed (class timezone is now immutable at creation; `admin.settings` became `admin.customizations`; `admin.economy_health` became `admin.economic_engine`), and 2 remain genuinely open: the dashboard's class list still queries `ClassEconomy`/`Seat` inline while `get_all_classes_by_teacher()` sits imported and uncalled, and the rent settings page has its FEAT boundaries but still assembles policy and cycle display in the route, importing three private helpers from the student blueprint. MAP-UI-001 v0.7 records the evidence per row.
- **Documentation serving split: help centre in the app, technical docs on the site (2026-09-17)** — `docs/user-guides` is the in-app help centre the application renders at `/docs`; every other directory under `docs/` is developer-facing and belongs to the technical site published from its own Pages branch, along with the four public pages, which sit outside the generator. The boundary is now decided in `app/utils/helpers.py` (`is_user_guide_doc_path`, `APP_OWNED_DOCS_ROUTES`) and shared by the docs blueprint and the URL builders, so a link cannot disagree with the route it targets. It was previously read from `docs-site/route-map.json`, which lists `user-guides` as migrated and returns `""` for the index — so setting `EXTERNAL_DOCS_BASE_URL` would have redirected the working in-app help centre, and its index, off the application. The operator audience toggle is removed: technical docs are no longer rendered in-app, so the switch could only produce a second, staler view of the same files, and in-app search now indexes the help centre only. SOP-DOC-000 v3.2 records the model in a new §X and drops "logs" from its governed categories, which contradicted its own removal notice.
- **SOP namespace renumbered from 001 in each subdivision (2026-09-17)** — After quarantining the superseded documents, the survivors were left at arbitrary numbers (a live-test runbook at 022, an index at 002). Each subdivision now numbers from 001: SOP-DB-011→001 (migration specifications), SOP-DB-014→002 (deprecated symbols), SOP-DB-015→003 (schema-change gate), SOP-DEP-022→001 (live-test runbook), SOP-DEP-023→002 (production transition), SOP-DOC-002→001 (documentation index). Renaming the index to SOP-DOC-001 also repairs a dangling citation in INV-CORE-000, which already referenced "SOP-DOC-000 and SOP-DOC-001". SOP-CORE-000 v1.2 records the mapping and corrects its subdivision list, which advertised `DOCUMENTATION/` and `WORKFLOW/` directories that never existed in v2 while omitting `SECURITY/` and `TESTING/`; it also states that numbers belonging to archived v1 documents may be reused, since those identifiers are retired. Every live reference was updated; citations inside merged migrations and older CHANGELOG entries keep their original identifiers as historical record, resolvable through the mapping table.
- **SOP namespace audited; six documents quarantined and the live-test runbook rewritten (2026-09-17)** — Every SOP was checked claim by claim against the code. Quarantined to `docs/archive/STANDARD_OPERATING_PROCEDURES/` (same subdirectory layout): SOP-DB-009, which cited three migration revisions absent from the graph (`e8f1a2b3c4d5`, `a11213ca4afb`, `2bde3e5f00ac`) and rested its readiness verdict on a deleted `docs/LOGS/` artifact; SOP-DB-010, which directed operators at a nonexistent `scripts/switch-db.sh` and described a `.env` safety guard that behaves the opposite way; SOP-DEP-021, whose source asset `static/images/brand-logo.svg` was deleted in #686; SOP-DEV-002a and its byte-duplicate SOP-DEV-002a-1, both signed records of one completed 2026-07-25 audit whose findings are fixed; and SOP-DEV-003, a playbook for a finished program citing a Phase 10 checklist that does not exist. SOP-DEP-022 was rewritten as v2.0 from `docs/TRACKING/PRODUCTION_FIRST_TEST_DEPLOYMENT_CHECKLIST_2026-09.md`: v1.2 described `ClassMembership`/`TeacherBlock` seeding, a fixed 664-test baseline, a `join_code` form of the student switch-class route, a `/health/deep` endpoint that now 404s by design, and record storage under the removed `docs/LOGS/`. The rewrite also records that the background scheduler starts per worker with no leader election, so a first test deployment runs a single worker. Verified current and kept: SOP-DEV-002, SOP-OPS-001, SOP-TEST-001, SOP-TEST-003, SOP-SEC-001, and SOP-DB-015.
- **Public policy wording (2026-09-15)** — Removed internal audit narration and claims about unfinished controls from the district and privacy pages. The internal audit retains the findings for follow-up; the public pages describe account use, data handling, and support.

- **Public district and privacy descriptions reconciled with v2 code (2026-09-15)** — Corrected authentication, hashing, shared accounts, claim retention, recovery, deletion, administrative access, exports, hall-pass disclosure, cookies, and external-service claims. Both pages distinguish direct identifiers, pseudonymous records, support observation, and separate application/infrastructure retention. Review corrected the teacher-recovery finding: one student per class matches DOM-IDEN-003; the all-students FEAT wording is stale. The audit separates disclosure corrections, normative ambiguity, source-level control mismatches, and operational unknowns instead of treating them as equivalent defects. The evidence and launch review findings are recorded in `docs/TRACKING/PUBLIC_PRIVACY_DISTRICT_AUDIT_20260915.md`. Existing section links and layout are preserved; structural checks passed for both pages, and the rendered public-page axe audit passed. No new accessibility violations were found.

### Security

- **The operator console's signed IAP assertion check never ran (2026-09-14)** — `status_service/identity.py::authenticated_operator_email` verified `X-Goog-IAP-JWT-Assertion` by calling `google.auth.jwt.decode(..., certs_url=..., request=...)`. `decode` accepts neither keyword in google-auth 2.40.3 (the pinned version) or in 2.58.0, so every call raised `TypeError`, a bare `except Exception` turned that into "no claims", and the assertion path authenticated nobody. Operators could get in only through the `IAP_TRUSTED_EMAIL_HEADER=true` fallback. It failed closed — nothing was accepted that should not have been — but the check the module's docstring describes did not exist.

  Verification now calls `google.auth.jwt.decode(assertion, certs=keys, audience=audience)` against IAP's `{kid: PEM}` key set. That is the supported form of the call the old code got wrong, and the same call `google.oauth2.id_token.verify_token` makes internally for this key format. It checks the signature, `exp`/`iat` and the audience. The `iss == https://cloud.google.com/iap` check and the operator allowlist are unchanged, and so is the trusted-header fallback. The `except` is narrowed to verification failures (`ValueError`, `GoogleAuthError`), so a defect in the call now surfaces instead of reading as a rejected operator — the exact shape that hid this one. Narrowing it exposed two crashes reachable from the header value. First, google-auth picks its verifier from the token's own header, and a header claiming `RS256` hands IAP's EC key to the RSA verifier, which raises `TypeError` rather than rejecting. Second, google-auth looks the `kid` up in the key set, so a `kid` that is a list or object raises `TypeError` as an unhashable value. IAP signs only with ES256 and always sends a string key id, so the algorithm is pinned and non-string key ids are rejected before verification.

  Covered by `tests/test_status_identity.py`, which signs ES256 assertions locally and serves the key set through a stubbed transport. It asserts acceptance for an allowlisted operator; rejection for a wrong audience, wrong issuer, non-allowlisted email, expired token, malformed tokens, a forged signature, a header naming another algorithm, and an unreachable key endpoint; that a programming error propagates; and that the header fallback is honoured when enabled, still allowlisted, and ignored when disabled. Every rejection case first proves an untampered assertion is accepted in the same setup, because against the old code, which rejected everything, a bare negative case passes vacuously. Thirteen of the seventeen cases were watched failing against the unfixed module; the four header-fallback cases pass on both, as they should. Each check was then removed in turn — the catch-all `except`, the algorithm pin, audience, issuer, expiry, allowlist, signature, the `ValueError` catch, the header gate — and each removal turned its own test red. `deploy-status.yml` now runs the file: its path filter already matched `tests/test_status_*.py`, but the test step names files explicitly, so a new file there would otherwise never run in CI.

  IAP's key set is now cached in the process rather than fetched on every verification, which is what `verify_token` does and what a CodeRabbit review flagged. `CacheControl` was considered and not used. It would add a dependency before launch, and for a token naming a key IAP had just rotated in, it would hand back the cached response, so rotation would still need custom invalidation. The cache holds one entry.
  - **Lifetime:** the endpoint's `Cache-Control: max-age` less its `Age`. The endpoint serves `max-age=3000` through a CDN, so `Age` is routinely non-zero, and ignoring it would keep keys past their freshness. `no-store`, `no-cache` or no `max-age` means nothing is cached.
  - **Rotation:** an assertion naming a key the entry lacks forces one fetch before it is rejected, so a rotation takes effect at once. At worst, a run of unknown key ids fetches once per request, which is what every verification cost before.
  - **Failure:** a failed fetch is neither cached nor papered over with the stale set. The assertion is rejected and the next request retries.
  - **Timeout:** fetches carry a 10-second timeout, because they hold the cache lock and google-auth's default is 120 seconds.

  `verify_token` fetches its own keys and has no seam for a cache, which is why verification calls `jwt.decode` directly. `tests/test_status_identity.py` runs the cache on a fake clock and now has 29 cases. The new ones cover reuse while fresh, refetch once stale, `Age`, the three uncacheable forms, a forced refetch for an unknown key id, a single refetch for one that stays unknown, failing closed and retrying after a failed refresh, the timeout, and non-string key ids. Fourteen mutations were each confirmed to turn their own test red: no caching, never expiring, no forced refetch, ignoring `Age` or `no-cache`, a default lifetime without `max-age`, caching a failed fetch, and removing the key-id guard, the algorithm pin, the timeout, the narrowed `except`, or the audience, issuer or signature check. The non-string key-id crash was reproduced against the previous module before the fix.

  Decided to land before launch, with the header fallback left on. **The status service must not launch until the operator service's environment has been verified.** `deploy-status.yml` deployed both Cloud Run services with `--set-env-vars` / `--set-secrets`, which per the gcloud reference remove every env var and secret they do not list. It listed none of `IAP_AUDIENCE`, `STATUS_OPERATOR_ALLOWLIST` or `IAP_TRUSTED_EMAIL_HEADER`, and none of them live in the repository — the `production` GitHub environment holds none either — so the first deploy to succeed would have emptied the allowlist, and both auth paths would then refuse every operator. Both deploys now use `--update-env-vars` / `--update-secrets`, which leave unlisted configuration in place. The public service gets the same change: `STATUS_CAPABILITIES` and `STATUS_PLATFORM_CHECKS` have defaults, but a value set on the service would have been silently reset to them.

  `--update-*` preserves configuration; it cannot restore configuration that is already missing, and a freshly created service has none. So a final deploy step describes `cth-status-operator` and fails the job when `STATUS_OPERATOR_ALLOWLIST` or `IAP_AUDIENCE` is absent or blank. A Secret Manager reference only names a secret, and a secret with a blank payload configures nothing, so for either value the step reads the payload and confirms it is not blank. A secret the job cannot read fails as unverified rather than passing, which means the deploy service account needs `secretmanager.versions.access` on any secret used this way. The step reports what the service actually carries, not what the deploy intended. It prints names and states only, never a value or payload, because this repository's Actions logs are public and the allowlist is email addresses. `tests/test_status_deploy_workflow.py` rejects the five replacing flags (`--set-env-vars`, `--set-secrets`, `--env-vars-file`, `--clear-env-vars`, `--clear-secrets`) on either deploy, after first asserting that both deploys were found, so a renamed service cannot make that check pass having inspected nothing. It also runs the check step's own script against described services, with `gcloud` stubbed to serve secret payloads: configured, populated secret, blank secret, unreadable secret, stripped by a `--set-env-vars` deploy, no allowlist, blank audience, and a service whose values, a secret's payload included, must not appear in the output. Against the workflow as it stood it fails 11 of 14 cases; the three passing are the flags that workflow never used. Each of the following turns its own test red: reverting either `--update-*` flag, trusting a secret reference without reading it, ignoring a blank payload or a blank direct value, treating an unreadable secret as configured, printing the environment or a payload, or exiting 0 unconditionally.

  **No status deploy has run yet.** The workflow's only push-triggered run, on 2026-09-07, was rejected before its first step: the `production` environment's deployment branch policy allowed only `codex/v2.0`, the name retired that day, and it blocked `release-v2.yml`, `toggle-maintenance.yml` and `tailscale-ssh-smoke-test.yml` from `main` the same way. The owner retargeted the policy to `main` on 2026-09-15. The next push to `main` that touches the status paths therefore deploys both services. Until this change is on `main`, that push would still run the old `--set-env-vars` workflow. CI has wiped nothing so far — whatever the two Cloud Run services carry was set by hand, and it has not been inspected. The launch gate, and what clears it, is recorded in `docs/TRACKING/PRODUCTION_READINESS_2026-09.md`.

- **Docs-site dependency advisories cleared to the ecosystem floor (2026-09-12)** — Seven of the nine open Dependabot advisories against `docs-site/package-lock.json` (`svgo` ×2, `joi` ×2, `qs` ×2, `colord`) are resolved by the `overrides` block and regenerated lockfile on this branch: `svgo` 3.3.5, `joi` 17.13.7, `qs` 6.16.0, `colord` 2.10.0. All four are transitive under `@docusaurus/*`; pinning them through `overrides` is the mechanism Docusaurus documents for transitive advisories, and it needs no Docusaurus version change. Verified with `npm ci` followed by `npm run build`: the site builds clean at 30 pages, so nothing was traded for the patches.

  The remaining two (`image-size`, both high, DoS via infinite loops in the ICNS and JXL/HEIF parsers) **cannot be patched today and no lockfile change will close them.** The advisory range is `<= 2.0.2`, and 2.0.2 is the newest version ever published, so every release of the package is in range — there is no version to move to. It arrives as `@docusaurus/core` 3.10.2 → `@docusaurus/mdx-loader` → `image-size@^2.0.2`, and 3.10.2 is the current Docusaurus release, so there is no upstream bump either. The package is effectively unmaintained: last code commit 2025-04-02, with no open issue or PR for either advisory. These two also account for all 18 entries `npm audit` reports — the other 16 are Docusaurus packages listed only as "depends on vulnerable versions of", with `image-size` as the sole root.

  Reachability, recorded so the residual risk is a measured one rather than an assumed one: `image-size` runs at **build time only**, invoked by `mdx-loader` to resolve dimensions for images referenced from markdown. It is not part of the Flask application, is absent from the served static output, and never sees visitor input. `docs-site/` currently contains **no image assets at all and no markdown image references**, so the ICNS/JXL/HEIF parsers are not merely hard to reach but unreachable. Exploitation would require an attacker with commit access to add a malicious `.icns`/`.jxl`/`.heif` file, whose entire effect would be to hang our own docs build.

  Those two alerts are therefore dismissed on reachability, and the dismissal carries its own invalidation condition: *reassess when Docusaurus or `image-size` publishes an updated dependency, or if documentation image processing is introduced.* Because "zero images" is load-bearing in that argument, it is enforced rather than remembered. `tests/test_docs_site_image_advisory_guard.py` fails if `docs-site/` gains an image asset or a markdown image reference while the lockfile still carries an `image-size` inside the advisory range, and the failure names the advisory to reassess. The guard reads the lockfile, so it retires itself the moment a patched `image-size` lands — it cannot outlive the risk it exists to track. It was confirmed to fail on an introduced image and reference, not merely to pass today. `image-size` sniffs format from magic bytes rather than file extension, so the guard covers all image assets rather than only the three affected formats.

- **Admin login accepted an attacker-supplied redirect target (2026-09-12)** — `admin.login` read `?next=` and passed it straight to `redirect()` with no validation at either of its two success branches. Any absolute URL was honoured, so a genuine CTH login flow could terminate on an attacker-controlled host. To be precise about the impact: an open redirect does not itself disclose the passphrase or TOTP code to the destination — the credentials are submitted to CTH and never reach the attacker's server. The harm is that a trusted login flow ends somewhere untrusted, which lends CTH's origin to convincing phishing, credential re-prompting, and malicious follow-on flows. This was a live open redirect, not a scanner artifact.

  The shared `is_safe_url` helper had a second, independent defect: it did not normalize backslashes before parsing. Browsers resolve `/\evil.example` and `\/evil.example` as protocol-relative URLs to `evil.example`, while `urlparse` reads them as local paths, so all three backslash forms returned `True`. Every caller of the helper inherited the bypass — student, sysadmin and `app/auth.py` redirect paths included — so the fix went into the helper rather than into the two routes a scanner happened to flag.

  `safe_redirect_target(target, fallback)` in `app/utils/helpers.py` is now the single way a `?next=` value becomes a redirect. It normalizes backslashes, then *rejects* rather than sanitizes anything bearing a scheme, a netloc, a protocol-relative prefix, or a non-root-relative path, returning the trusted fallback instead; in-app paths keep their query string and fragment. `admin.login` and `main.switch_view` now route through it, and `is_safe_url` normalizes backslashes for its remaining callers.

  Covered by `tests/test_open_redirect_hardening.py` (22 cases). The pre-fix behaviour was reproduced directly first: the old helper returned `True` for all three backslash forms, and the old admin branch returned `https://evil.example/phish` verbatim.

- **Roster sync returned raw exception text to the browser (2026-09-12)** — The per-row `except` in the roster sync endpoint appended `str(e)` to an `errors` list that is serialized into the JSON response. Row failures there are mostly `SQLAlchemy` errors, whose text carries schema detail and the bound parameters — which on this endpoint are student first and last names, i.e. exactly the PII `INV-ARC-005` keeps out of unencrypted surfaces. The handler now logs via `logger.exception` (keeping the traceback server-side, where it is useful) and returns `"Row N: Could not be processed."`. The row number, which is the only part a teacher can act on, is preserved.

- **A debit could authorize against a balance that silently omitted another debit (2026-09-11, #1364)** — Debit FEATs serialized on the `seats` row, but settlement never took that lock, so a debit and a settlement for the same seat did not serialize at all. `get_available_balance` was also two statements, posted then pending, and a settlement committing between them moved a pending debit out of the pending term after the posted term had already been read. The debit was counted in neither term, the sufficiency check passed, and the seat could overdraw. Because posted rows are immutable (`INV-LED-002`), that overdraft could not be corrected by rewriting history.

  Settlement now locks the seat row before the class row. `pay_rent` and `execute_purchase_insurance`, which checked affordability without any lock, now take the seat lock before reading the balance and hold it through the debit. `get_available_balance` and `get_available_balances` evaluate posted and pending as scalar subqueries of one statement, so the two terms always come from the same database snapshot. `INV-LED-015` in `DOM-LED-001` now states which row serializes a seat's money and in what order the locks are taken.

  Lock-order audit: transfer, store purchase and direct grant already take the seat lock first. No path takes the class row before the seat. `insurance_claim_feat` locks only `EntitlementEvent` rows and reads no balance. The only live caller of ledger resolution is store purchase, which already holds the seat lock. `apply_overdraft_fee_if_needed` has no callers.

  Covered by `tests/dom/ledger/test_seat_balance_serialization.py`. It holds the seat lock on a second connection and shows settlement cannot proceed, with the snapshot rows created first so the foreign key's `FOR KEY SHARE` lock cannot make it pass by accident. It also asserts the lock order, that rent and insurance payments lock before reading, and that an available-balance read is a single statement. Each test was watched failing with its fix removed.

  Taking the lock also meant replay resolution had to move under it. A same-key retry that waits on the seat lock acts on whatever it read before waiting. `execute_purchase_insurance` looked up "this command already granted" before the lock, so a retry that waited out its own first attempt fell through to the coverage check and reported `POLICY_ALREADY_HELD` for its own purchase. It now takes the seat lock first. `pay_rent` had a worse replay defect, and one that occurred even with no concurrency. A same-key partial-payment replay counted its own committed debit in `paid_before`, got the same ledger row back from the idempotent write, and reported the payment a second time with a remaining balance that was never paid. For example, replaying a 20.00 payment on a 50.00 bill reported 10.00 remaining instead of 30.00. The replay now resolves from the command's own ledger row, under the lock, and reports the original payment. Three more tests cover this: both replay lookups run after the seat lock, and a partial rent replay reports the original amount and writes nothing. Each was watched failing with its fix removed.

- **Student PII no longer reaches the application log (2026-09-09)** — The failure path in `admin.delete_student` logged `identity_profile.full_name`, putting a decrypted student name into an unencrypted, off-host-shipped log in violation of `INV-ARC-005`. It now logs `seat_id` and uses `logger.exception`, so the traceback is kept and the student is not. The flash messages still name the student: those are shown to the authenticated teacher who owns the class, which is authorized display, not logging.

- **CSRF token no longer reaches the application log (2026-09-09)** — The same route logged `dict(request.form)` wholesale on entry, which included the session-bound `csrf_token`. It now records the sorted field *names* only, keeping the diagnostic without the secret. Both leaks are covered by `tests/dom/identity/test_student_deletion_log_hygiene.py`, which asserts against real emitted log records and fails if either value returns.

- **Removed a developer's local filesystem path from the public repository (2026-09-09)** — 129 occurrences of an absolute `/Users/<name>/...` path across five documents in `docs/` disclosed a maintainer's real name and local directory layout. Paths are now repository-relative, which also repairs the broken absolute markdown links in the archived route audits.

### Removed

- **Seven dead templates deleted (2026-09-23)** — `admin_view_student_policy.html`, `student/recovery/identity_update.html`, `student/recovery/reset_form.html`, `student/recovery/landing.html`, `system_admin_error_logs.html`, `system_admin_logs_testing.html`, and `system_admin_network_activity.html` had zero `render_template()` references anywhere in `app/` and no `{% extends %}`/`{% include %}` from any surviving template — confirmed during the WCAG accessibility sweep's Group I template mapping. `admin_view_student_policy.html`'s own route (`admin.view_student_policy`) carries `@admin_required` but no route decorator, so it was already absent from the URL map (tracked separately in `docs/MAP/MAP-UI-001_TEMPLATE_TO_FEAT_WIRING_MAP.md`); the three sysadmin log pages were superseded by `sysadmin_combined_logs.html` per `combined_logs()`'s own docstring ("Replaces the separate error-logs and network-activity pages"), and their routes (`/error-logs`, `/logs-testing`, `/network-activity`) now just redirect there and are left untouched. `student/recovery/layout.html` (a shared base template) and `student/recovery/account_lookup.html` (rendered from `app/routes/recovery.py:126`) are unaffected. Also fixed a stale docstring in `app/services/operational_event_service.py` that named the now-deleted `system_admin_logs_testing.html` as a consumer instead of the actual current one, `sysadmin_combined_logs.html`.

- **`bonus_rate` deleted from payroll settings (2026-09-10)** — A v1 artifact that never became a feature: a Float column on `PayrollSettings`, a form field labelled "Bonus Rate ($ per minute)" on `PayrollSettingsForm`, and membership in `_FROZEN_POLICY_FIELDS`. Nothing else. No template rendered it, no route, service or FEAT read it, no test asserted it, and no document under `docs/INVARIANT/`, `docs/DOMAIN/`, `docs/FEATURE-EXECUTION/`, `docs/SPEC/` or `docs/STANDARD_OPERATING_PROCEDURES/` has ever required it. A teacher could type a number into it and the number went nowhere.

  That is worse than an unfinished feature. `payroll_settings` is an append-only versioned policy table (`DOM-POL-001` §VI.1), so every row is a contract the teacher is told they submitted — and a frozen field that silently means nothing puts a fictional term inside an immutable contract.

  Dropping a column from an immutable-policy table destroys what historical rows asserted, so this was checked against real data first: every existing row held `0.0`, the column default, so no policy version loses a term it actually carried. Migration `a4f2b7c91d38`; upgrade/downgrade/re-upgrade verified. Because `_SUBMITTABLE_FIELDS` in `payroll_settings_service` is derived from `_FROZEN_POLICY_FIELDS`, a caller still passing `bonus_rate` now raises rather than silently writing a column that no longer exists.

- **Prohibited version pointer dropped from `payroll_settings` (2026-09-10)** — `payroll_settings.policy_version_id`, its index and its foreign key into `policy_versions` are an alternative version pointer on a Policies repository table. `DOM-POL-001` §VI.0 prohibits that construct by name — "whether a self-referential FK on a Policies table or an external version-tracking table" — and says explicitly that `policy_versions` / `policy_transitions` record economic-policy evolution and are not a domain-policy versioning mechanism.

  The column arrived 2026-07-19 (`c3d4e5f8a9b`), when payroll settings still tracked lineage by pointing at `policy_versions`. The 2026-09-04 append-only conversion (`3bb29ef4e874`) made `policy_uuid` the version and dropped `is_active` for exactly this reason — its own docstring calls two current-policy projections "exactly the alternative version pointer DOM-POL-001 §VI.0 prohibits" — but walked past the second pointer. `rent_settings` and `hall_pass_settings`, converted in the same migration, carry no such column; `payroll_settings` was the outlier.

  Nothing could break: `PayrollSettings` never declared the column, so no route, service, FEAT, test or template ever read or wrote it, and all six rows held `NULL`. Migration `b7d3e2064c15`; upgrade/downgrade/re-upgrade verified, FK discovered by column rather than by hardcoded name. `payroll_event.policy_version_id` is deliberately untouched — `PayrollEvent` is an operational fact in Productivity, not a Policies row, and freezing lineage on a historical fact is the contract (`FEAT-PROD-003` fails closed without it).

### Added

- **The operator console logs which path authenticated each request (2026-09-15)** — The console accepts an operator by the signed IAP assertion or, through a fallback left on for launch, by the email header IAP sets. Nothing recorded which one let a request in. That made a wrong `IAP_AUDIENCE` invisible: every signed assertion would be rejected quietly while the header let operators in anyway. Deploy run 34929999750 showed `IAP_AUDIENCE` is set and not blank, but not that it is right. `status_service/identity.py` now logs one line per operator request, and one real sign-in answers the question:
  - `status operator authenticated via=iap_assertion` — the audience is right;
  - `status operator authenticated via=trusted_header assertion=<reason>` — the header admitted the operator, and `assertion=wrong_audience` means the audience is wrong;
  - `status operator refused assertion=<reason> header=<state>`.

  Reasons are a fixed set of codes: `absent`, `audience_not_configured`, `wrong_algorithm`, `invalid_key_id`, `key_fetch_failed`, `invalid_token`, `wrong_audience`, `wrong_issuer` and `not_allowlisted`. Neither the email nor the token is logged, and neither is exception text, because google-auth's messages can quote the token. To give a mismatch its own code without parsing those messages, `identity.py` compares the audience itself, after `jwt.decode` has verified the signature and expiry; every token gets the same accept-or-reject result as before. A key-set response that is not a JSON object now counts as `key_fetch_failed`.

  The service previously had no logging configuration, and under gunicorn Python's last-resort handler drops INFO. `status_service/log_setup.py::configure_logging` therefore gives the `status_service` logger a stderr handler at INFO, and `create_app` calls it. `tests/test_status_identity.py` covers each line and reason, header refusals, an empty allowlist, that no email or token reaches the log text, that INFO reaches stderr, and that `create_app` configures logging. Twelve mutations were each confirmed to turn their own test red: removing the audience comparison, the signature check, the algorithm pin, the key-id guard, the issuer check or the narrowed `except`; dropping the header line's reason or the `not_allowlisted` reason; logging the email; folding fetch failures into `invalid_token`; or unwiring either half of the logging setup.

- **`SPEC-DES-001` supersedes the archived `FEAT-DES-001` as the design authority (2026-09-11)** — The design system's only written description was `FEAT-DES-001` v1.1, archived and marked non-normative, and it had drifted: it documents the teacher accent as `#d3af37` and the student accent as bronze `#ac8255`, while the implementation converged all three roles on `#D4A857`. A design system with no live normative document cannot be conformed to, and three separate defects below existed precisely because no rule forbade them.

  The new spec records the canonical brand values, requires the scales v1 omitted (spacing, type, motion, opacity, icon sizing — v1 tokenized only color and radius, against 25 distinct font sizes and 19 spacing values hardcoded in `style.css`), states the Bootstrap `--bs-*-rgb` parity rule, distinguishes a legitimate Jinja-computed inline style from a static violation, binds contrast obligations to `INV-ARC-020`, and adds machine-checkable conformance criteria. It is filed Normative Tier 2 and cites the archived predecessor inline rather than in Dependencies, per `SOP-DOC-000`.

### Changed

- **Launch-window dependency patch batch (2026-09-14)** — Takes the low-risk half of the 2026-09-14 Dependabot run as one verified change: `SQLAlchemy` 2.0.52, `psycopg2-binary` 2.9.13, `cryptography` 50.0.1, `greenlet` 3.5.5, `markdown` 3.10.3, `soupsieve` 2.9.2 and `python-dotenv` 1.2.3 (#1373), `zope.interface` 8.6 (#1382), `Pygments` 2.21.0 (#1379), `playwright` 1.62.0 (#1377), and `actions/upload-artifact` v7 in the Constitutional CI and Full Test Suite workflows (#1370). The Python bumps are low-risk patch and compatibility updates, and none changes an API the application calls. `SQLAlchemy` 2.0.52 also fixes ORM `UPDATE … RETURNING` rows being keyed to the wrong column under `synchronize_session="fetch"`, and `cryptography` 50.0.1 only rebuilds its wheels against OpenSSL 4.0.2. `upload-artifact` v7 keeps every input these workflows pass (`name`, `path`, `if-no-files-found`, `retention-days`) and moves the action to Node 24.

  The Dependabot PRs themselves carried no test evidence. Dependabot-triggered runs receive only Dependabot secrets, so Constitutional CI's `ENCRYPTION_KEY` arrived empty and collection crashed before any test ran. Verification was therefore local, on Python 3.10 (the production runtime) against an isolated database: the full suite ran 2,748 passed, 15 skipped, 4 failed. Those 4 failures (`test_store_surface_no_rent_panel.py` ×3 and `test_store_products_consolidation_is_idempotent`) reproduce identically on `main` at `1cc526049` with its current pins, so they predate this change. The Playwright browser tests (`test_store_item_type_gating_browser.py` and `test_axe_compliance.py`, 6 tests) actually ran rather than skipped, and pass on 1.62.0.

  Deliberately **not** included, and staged on `claude/deps-risky-upgrades` for after launch: `alembic` 1.19.2, `Mako` 1.4.1, `gunicorn` 26.2.0, `redis` 8.1.0, `google-auth` 2.58.0, `google-cloud-firestore` 2.30.0, and `google-github-actions/auth` and `setup-gcloud` v3. #1361 (docs-site `colord`/`joi`/`svgo`) was closed as superseded because `main` already carried those versions.

- **`SOP-DB-011` v1.1: one named exception to "never edit merged migrations" (2026-09-14)** — Golden Rule 3 still says never, and still means it. New §V.A defines the single circumstance in which following it literally would preserve an execution path that destroys state protected by an `INV` or `DOM` document: a migration whose existence checks cannot tell its historical input from its own output. Such a migration may receive a Replay-Safety Correction that makes it strictly less destructive on replay, but only when all six conditions are proven:
  1. its replay can destroy, rewrite or invalidate `INV`/`DOM`-protected state, with the protecting clause cited;
  2. no forward corrective migration can eliminate the behavior;
  3. its behavior against its intended predecessor schema is unchanged;
  4. a fresh upgrade produces the same intended schema and data transformation;
  5. replay and re-upgrade prove the destructive behavior is gone;
  6. the exception and its production risk are recorded under `SOP-DB-015` §IX.

  The section requires execution evidence for each condition (`INV-ARC-017` §V–§VI) and a comment in the corrected migration naming §V.A. It requires each candidate to be evaluated on its own evidence, with no correction serving as precedent for another. It prohibits using the exception to change a migration's intended schema, add application behavior, repair defects a later revision can fix, or avoid a corrective migration. `.claude/rules/database-migrations.md` and `.agent/rules/database-migration-best-practice.md` now point to it.

- **Economic reference bands recalibrated to `SPEC-ECON-003` v2.0 and reduced to one source each (2026-09-14)** — Four bands in `POLICY_MODES` had drifted from the spec's §4 tables and are now transcribed from them: weekly rent (§4.3) is 30–40% / 35–50% / 40–55% of CWI across tight / default / comfortable, fines (§4.5) are 5–10% / 5–12% / 7–15%, collective goals (§4.6) are 1–3× / 1–5× / 1.5–7×, and the comfortable weekly savings target (§4.2) is 15%, not the 17.5% the code carried. Note that rent and fines now read *higher* in comfortable than in tight; §4.3, §4.5 and the §8 summary table all state it that way.

  `SPEC-ECON-003` §4.5.1 and §8 were clarified in the same pass. §4.5.1 opened by saying internal fines "shall use the same formula as generic classroom fines recommendations" and §8 said overdraft fees "shall use the above table for reference", but §4.5.1's own progressive tier table does not fit inside the §4.5 band — tight escalates to 18% against a 5–10% band, and comfortable opens at 4% against a 7% floor — and it orders the modes the other way round, biting hardest under tight. Read together, those sentences invited an implementer to clamp the tiers into the band, which would flatten the repeat-occurrence escalation the schedule exists to express. The spec now states that a *flat* internal fee is priced against the §4.5 band while the *progressive* schedule is a self-contained reference set that MUST NOT be clamped or validated against it, and §8's sentence is scoped to the flat shape. No numbers changed; `_PROGRESSIVE_FINE_TIERS` stays independent of `POLICY_MODES` by design, now carrying a comment saying why. `test_progressive_tiers_are_not_clamped_to_the_generic_fine_band` asserts each mode keeps at least one tier outside the band — precisely what clamping would erase. The insurance premium row's inversion (tight 6–14%, comfortable 4–10%, the one §8 row where tight is harsher) is likewise deliberate and was left alone.

  The same band was spelled in up to three places. `EconomyBalanceChecker` carried `RENT_*`/`FINE_*`/`MIN_WEEKLY_SAVINGS_RATIO` fallback constants whose values bore no relation to the policy table they were meant to back up (`RENT_MIN_RATIO = 2.0` against a 0.35 band), and `economic_engine.py` kept its own `_FINE_BANDS` and `_SAVINGS_RATES` dicts. The fallbacks are unreachable — `normalize_policy_mode` guarantees a declared mode — so they could only ever drift, and `_FINE_BANDS` already had: the engine judged an overdraft fee against tight 7–18% while the settings page recommended tight 5–10% for the same class. All of them now read `POLICY_MODES` (`INV-ARC-022`), as do the three `collective_goal` fallbacks inlined in `app/routes/admin.py`.

  Collapsing them surfaced a rounding divergence the duplication had hidden: `economy_policy._quantize_money` used the default half-to-even context while the engine's `_money` used half-up, so a band landing on an exact half-cent (CWI 337.50 at 7%) came out a cent apart between the two. Both are half-up now.

  It also surfaced a fourth restatement, in `app/services/store/form_contract.py`. The collective-goal guidance line on both Store item forms carried its own `{'min': 1.0, 'max': 8.0}` literal, and `1×–8×` is a range no policy mode defines — not before this pass and not after. Because the route already resolves the class's real band, the text a teacher read while naming a goal amount was not the band the Economic Engine would judge that goal against, which is the exact failure mode `INV-ARC-022` exists to prevent. The literal is gone; an omitted band now resolves `get_policy_profile(None)`, which normalizes to `POLICY_MODE_DEFAULT`, so the fallback is a read from the one source rather than a second spelling of it.

  That guidance is now rendered currency-first: `Recommended range: $337.50–$1,687.50 (1×–5× CWI)`. A teacher enters a goal in dollars, so a bare ratio asked them to do the arithmetic the surface exists to do for them — and a ratio-only string is also what let the drifted `1×–8×` sit unnoticed, since nothing on screen contradicted it. The multiplier stays alongside the dollars because §4.6 specifies the ratio and Rebalance re-derives from it. Where a class has no pay rate or expected weekly hours there is no CWI to price against, and the hint now says so rather than presenting the bare ratio as the recommendation. `test_collective_goal_guidance_quotes_currency_from_the_policy_band` pins the dollar endpoints to `cwi × POLICY_MODES[mode]`, so the two halves cannot drift apart again.

  `utilities_weekly` is removed from all three modes, along with the `"utilities"` key in `get_price_recommendation_context` and the dead `UTILITIES_*_RATIO` constants. v2 has no utilities feature and `SPEC-ECON-003` does not model one; no runtime consumer read either. No replacement was invented.

  Two new tests in `tests/test_pricing_recommendation_single_source.py` hold the collapse shut: the overdraft fee band must equal the band the fine settings page shows, and the interest ceiling's savings rate must equal the teacher-facing savings target. The first of these failed on the half-cent case before the rounding fix.

- **Store pricing tiers replaced by the declared economic role (2026-09-13)** — `SPEC-ECON-003` v2.0 §4.7 retires the four-tier Store price ladder (`basic`/`standard`/`premium`/`luxury`) in favour of three economic roles: Necessity (1–10% of CWI), Convenience (11–20%), Add-on (21–30%). This is a direction reversal, not a rename. The old tier was *inferred from the price* — the checker scanned the bands and reported whichever one happened to contain the number — so a product had no stable role to be judged against across a price change, and a teacher who raised a price silently reclassified the item. The role is now *declared by the teacher* and the price is positioned against it.

  The role is advisory. `DOM-STORE-001` §XII makes it configuration guidance rather than entitlement authority, and `SPEC-STORE-001` §IV.D carries overdue-purchase permission solely on `available_with_overdue_obligations`; `economic_role` selects a reference band and authorizes nothing. Per §4.7 a price outside its role's band "MUST remain valid", so an out-of-band price is reported at WARNING and never at CRITICAL — CRITICAL drives `is_valid: false` in `api_economy_validate`, which would have turned an advisory band into an enforcement gate.

  The §4.7 table has no policy-mode axis, so the bands are identical in tight, default and comfortable. The three per-mode `store_tiers` blocks in `POLICY_MODES`, the `_store_tiers()` builder, `_set_tier_choices()`, and the edit route's per-mode choice loop existed only to read those per-mode dicts and were deleted rather than ported. The band table now has one definition, `STORE_ROLE_RATIOS` in `economy_policy`, which `EconomyBalanceChecker.STORE_ROLE_BANDS` binds to rather than restates — so the band a teacher is shown on the Economic Engine card is the band the checker judges a price against (`INV-ARC-022`).

  `economic_role` is required on every item type, collective goals included, and `SPEC-STORE-001` §IV.A makes it non-nullable. A nullable column would have been exactly the compatibility bridge the append-only product model forbids, so migration `046dccd4451e` backfills inside the migration and lands the column `NOT NULL` with a check constraint, then drops `tier`. The backfill is deterministic (`basic`/`standard` → `necessity`, `premium` → `convenience`, `luxury` → `add_on`); the reverse mapping is lossy by construction, so downgrade restores the coarse tier rather than the original value. Upgrade/downgrade/re-upgrade verified, single head throughout.

  Covered by `TestEconomicRoleIsAdvisory` in `tests/test_store_policy_resolver.py` (a Necessity product is not implicitly essential; an Add-on product may be essential; every item type requires a role; non-canonical roles are rejected) and by `tests/test_pricing_recommendation_single_source.py`, which now asserts the bands do not vary by policy mode, that a price copied from a displayed band reads as inside it, and that an out-of-band price is reported rather than rejected.

- **README brought back in line with the code (2026-09-11)** — Every claim in `README.md` was checked against the repository. Removed claims nothing implements: OpenTelemetry instrumentation (the packages are pinned but nothing imports them), post-claim PII deletion (a v1 behaviour tied to removed models), sysadmin user provisioning and 2FA resets from the web portal (sysadmins are CLI-created only), and sysadmin announcements (announcements are teacher-side). Corrected the rest: sign-up is three steps, not two; pay rates are per minute, not hourly; seat claim matches a name against the roster rather than a teacher-issued credential; the retired Analytics module is now the read-only Interpretation report; the migration head is no longer a merge point; CI runs Python 3.10, 3.11 and 3.13 against PostgreSQL 15 and 16. Added the shipped features the README never named — insurance, banking and savings interest, the Economic Engine, ledger settlement and the scheduled jobs, passkeys, issues, announcements, feature settings, in-app docs, maintenance mode — plus the optional environment variables and the design-token checker. `CSRF_SECRET_KEY` moved out of the required setup, since the app falls back to `SECRET_KEY` without it.

- **Every template now conforms to the `SPEC-DES-001` template contract, and a gate keeps it that way (2026-09-11)** — An audit of all 103 templates under `templates/` found 1,156 violations in 72 of them: 146 hardcoded colours, 471 static inline `style` attributes, 496 literal design values in page `<style>` blocks, and 43 page selectors that `style.css` also defined. All are resolved. `docs-site/` is a separately published artifact (§XIII) and was not in scope; the in-app docs under `templates/docs/` were.

  The spec already said this was mechanically checkable and should be gated; nothing did. `scripts/lint_design_tokens.py` now checks §XIV rules 1, 5, 6 and 7, and `tests/test_design_token_contract.py` runs it with one test per rule, plus probes proving each rule still detects its violation and still admits tokens, structural geometry and id selectors — so the gate cannot pass by going blind. Run the script directly on templates you are editing.

  One way it could have gone blind anyway: the scanner delimited blocks on a literal `</style>` / `</script>`, but HTML permits whitespace and ignored attributes inside an end tag, so a block closed with `</style >` was invisible to it — and a `<style>` block that never matched is a block whose every violation goes unreported. Flagged by CodeQL as `py/bad-tag-filter`. Both patterns now accept the full end-tag form while still rejecting near-misses like `</styled>`, with probes for the whitespace variants; each probe was confirmed to go undetected under the previous pattern.

  Shared layer. The six error pages carried near-identical blocks the spec had listed as outstanding; their chrome is now one `body.error-page` section in `style.css`. New token utilities (`.type-*`, `.tracking-*`, `.alpha-*`, `.text-accent`, `.scroll-panel`, `.icon-2xs`, `.icon-3xl`) give templates one class per token instead of an inline style. New tokens: `--icon-2xs`, `--icon-3xl`, and display sizes `--text-5xl` / `--text-6xl`, so a status code no longer borrows an icon token for text. `SPEC-DES-001` is v2.1.

  Behaviour. Static `style="display:none"` became the `hidden` attribute across 15 templates, `static/js/attendance.js` and `static/js/item-form-economy.js`, with every script that toggled those elements moved to `el.hidden` — Bootstrap's `[hidden]` rule is `!important`, so a leftover `style.display = 'block'` would have silently stopped showing the element.

  Defects found on the way:
  - Four contrast failures: the select-class panel heading was dark text on the dark green panel; the maintenance page's "bug" badge was white on the gold fill (~2.1:1); the getting-started Skip button hover was white on gold (2.2:1); timeline tag text sat at ~4.4:1.
  - `student_detail.html` put its styles in `{% block extra_styles %}`, which `layout_admin.html` does not define, so they never rendered. It now uses `extra_head`.
  - `static/sw.js` precached `style.css` but not `tokens.css`, so the offline page lost every token exactly when the network was down. Cache version bumped to v9.
  - Dead page CSS removed where it matched nothing or always lost to a shared `!important` rule.

  Deliberate visual changes worth reviewing. With no matching token, some hues now reuse the nearest semantic one: in the docs, IMPORTANT and TIP callouts share the `--primary` family; the timeline's Security (orange) and Philosophy (purple) categories now render gold-brown and graphite; the student troubleshooting card header is the role primary rather than brown. The six error pages share one card treatment, so 404 and 500 lose their rounded corners and the others get `--shadow-lg`. Several floating shadows are softer because `--shadow-lg` is lighter than the hand-written ones it replaces. Other values moved at most one step to the nearest token.

- **Auth form chrome is defined once instead of fourteen times, and the dead copies are gone (2026-09-11)** — The fourteen auth templates carried 58 distinct selectors between them in page-scoped `<style>` blocks, 21 of them duplicated across pages with visible drift. The label, subtitle, help-text, code-input and footer-link rules are now defined once in `style.css` under `.auth-body`; seven templates carry no `<style>` block at all, and the ones that remain hold only genuinely page-specific chrome (QR panels, step indicators, the strength meter, the sysadmin security notice).

  Most of what was deleted had never rendered. Every page copy of `.btn-primary`, `.btn-outline-primary`, `.btn-outline-secondary`, `.text-primary` and the bare `a` / `a:hover` rules was already beaten by `style.css`, which claims those selectors with `!important` — and `!important` wins over any specificity. Three of the dead `.btn-primary:hover` copies had independently drifted to a fill that would have failed contrast had it ever applied, which is precisely the risk of an invisible rule: it is edited, reviewed and merged as if it were live. Confirmed rule by rule in-browser before removal, and `.btn-outline-danger` was kept because `style.css` does not claim it.

  One visible change: `.subtitle` was gold on some pages and neutral on others. It is now `--text-secondary` everywhere. Gold at body size on white is 2.12:1 and was never legible as prose — `--accent-text-on-light` exists for accent text and is what the uppercase field labels use. `templates/admin_recovery_saved.html` and `admin_recovery_status.html`, which sat outside the redesign, were tokenized in the same pass; both were still on literal `background: white`, `1.75rem`, `3rem` and `letter-spacing: 0.3em`, and both carried the same dead gradient buttons.

  Re-validated per `INV-ARC-020` §X: a per-element contrast sweep across all six reachable auth routes — every rendered text node measured against its effective background, at the AA threshold appropriate to its computed size and weight — reports zero failures in all three role shells. `pytest tests/test_accessibility.py tests/test_axe_compliance.py tests/test_layout_accessibility_contract.py` passes (97 passed, 14 skipped), and all 103 templates parse.

- **`SPEC-DES-001` now distinguishes a design value from a structural one (2026-09-11)** — §IX permitted a page-scoped `<style>` block only if every value in it resolved to a token, which no template could satisfy. `min-height: 100dvh`, `width: 0%` on a meter that script drives, `border-radius: 50%` on a circular step indicator, `grid-template-columns: 1fr 1fr auto` and a `max-width` capping a QR image are facts about one element's geometry, not decisions the design system owns; tokenizing them would mint tokens with a single call site each and make the scales meaningless.

  The rule now applies to design values — anything a token family could change system-wide — and admits structural literals with a falsifiable test: a value is structural only if changing the corresponding token everywhere would not be expected to change it, and when in doubt it is a design value. §XII conformance and §XIV enforcement were amended in the same edit so the criteria cannot be satisfied by a rule the criteria no longer match. §XIV also gains a rule against a selector appearing in both a page block and `style.css`, which is the defect the deletion above found and which produces no visible symptom.

- **The auth pages stack the brand above the form instead of setting it beside them (2026-09-11)** — Fourteen pages — three sign-in, the sign-up and credential flows, and the student recovery layout — each carried their own copy of a two-column split: form on the left, a `linear-gradient` panel on the right holding a 400×400px PNG logo. The panel was decorative only, and every copy ended in the same `@media (max-width: 768px) { .login-right { display: none } }`, so the brand vanished entirely on a phone — which is where much of the audience for a student sign-in page actually is.

  They now share one layout defined once in `style.css`: a full-width brand band on top (`.auth-brand`), the interactive panel beneath (`.auth-body`). The band is filled with `var(--primary)`, so it takes Advisor Green, Steward Blue, or Guardian Gray from the role class already on `<body>` — no per-page override and no role conditional. The brand inside it is the text wordmark in the same vertical stack the public landing hero uses, rendered from a new `macros/auth_brand.html` rather than transcribed fourteen times.

  The card width is pinned rather than inherited. These pages center the card as a flex item, so the previous `width: 100%` resolved against a shrink-to-fit parent and every page ended up as wide as its own longest line of copy — the teacher card measured 346px and the student card 457px on the same viewport, because the student subtitle is a longer sentence. All fourteen are now 400px. The narrow-screen cap is measured against the viewport, not the parent: `max-width: 100%` cannot rescue a flex item whose automatic minimum size is pinned by its specified width, so the card would have overflowed instead of shrinking. Checked down to a 320px viewport with no horizontal overflow.

  The three PNG logos are gone from the templates. An image logo cannot take a role theme, cannot be recolored by a token, and needs a redundant `alt` string; the wordmark answers all three and stays legible at every width. Net template change is −961 lines against +370, nearly all of it deleted duplication.

  Contrast validated per `INV-ARC-020` §X on all three bands: the white wordmark on `--primary` is 9.56:1 (teacher), 8.25:1 (student) and 13.20:1 (sysadmin), all clearing AA for normal text. The gold leading icons are decorative `aria-hidden` and clear the 3:1 large-text threshold at 4.34 / 3.75 / 5.99:1. Verified in-browser across all three role shells at desktop and 375px widths with no console errors. Recorded in `SPEC-DES-001` §IX under "The brand mark" and "The auth shell".

- **Design tokens now cover the scales the stylesheet was hardcoding (2026-09-11)** — Added spacing, type, weight, line-height, motion, opacity, icon-size, layout and breakpoint scales to `tokens.css`, plus `.icon-*` utilities so icon sizing stops being an inline `font-size`. Sysadmin aliases (`--sysadmin-primary`, `--sysadmin-primary-dark`, `--sysadmin-accent`, `--sysadmin-bottom-nav-height`) now alias the theme tokens instead of restating their literals, so they follow the theme when it moves rather than silently diverging from it.

  Remaining hardcoded colors were given token homes and replaced: the student shell's nav-active fill and brand hover accents, the alert contrast overrides, the sysadmin gradient and sign-out hover, and every bare `#fff`/`#5f5f5c`. `#000` inside `color-mix()` is left alone — it is a mixing endpoint, not a color choice. `templates/admin_dashboard.html` lost all seven inline `color:` styles in favor of `text-muted`.

### Fixed

- **Manual payments no longer require payroll setup (2026-09-17)** — A teacher's manual payment failed with a server error in any class without an active payroll policy, because every payroll event had to carry a policy version. Policy provenance now follows who decided the amount: attendance-derived `payroll` events still require a policy version (enforced by the new `ck_payroll_event_payroll_policy` check constraint), a teacher-entered manual credit needs none, and a manual credit posted for a policy calculation such as productivity insurance keeps its provenance. Reversals inherit the original event's provenance (DOM-PROD-001 v1.3, FEAT-PROD-003 v1.1).
- **Student deletion failures are handled and logged without names (2026-09-17)** — A failed roster deletion now rolls back, logs only seat IDs, and shows an error instead of an unhandled server error. Removes the unused `_remove_student_from_teacher_scope` route helper. The recovery status page's resume button declares its type.
- **Student detail rent status reflects the class rent feature (2026-09-16)** — The page labelled every student "Housing: Renter", always showed the Rent tab, and marked students overdue from a hard-coded 5th-of-month due date even with rent disabled. The Housing row is removed; the Rent tab appears only when rent is enabled for the class and reports the student's recorded rent assessment. The always-"Yes" Rent Enabled column is removed from the student export, and the unused `Seat.is_rent_enabled` property is gone (its database column remains).
- **Manual payment Apply button works again (2026-09-16)** — The payroll Manual Payments form referenced an undefined `saveAction` variable, so clicking Apply Payment threw a JavaScript error and sent nothing.
- **Re-applying two merged migrations destroyed protected insurance and support state; corrected under SOP-DB-011 §V.A (2026-09-14)** — Three candidates were each evaluated on their own evidence against the Replay-Safety Correction exception in SOP-DB-011 v1.1 §V.A. `7c3d4e5f6a7b` and `6b2c3d4e5f6a` qualify and are corrected. `0001_bootstrap` does not qualify and is unchanged.

  **`7c3d4e5f6a7b` qualifies.** Condition 1: its insurance group dropped `insurance_policies`, `insurance_claims` and `insurance_claim_productivity_dates` on bare existence. The v2 insurance domain re-creates all three names (`a7b8c9d0e1f3`, `b2c3d4e5f6a7`, `c3d4e5f6a7b8`), so a re-application deleted every insurance definition. DOM-POL-001 §VI.1 makes the Policies repository append-only and allows deleting a definition only once it is retired and its live dependencies have drained. DOM-CORE-002 §V.11 holds definition rows immutable after insert. Claims and claimed dates were dropped too. No INV or DOM clause protects the `insurance_claims` table as such, and DOM-STORE-001 §VI lists it among non-canonical concepts, so condition 1 rests on the definitions. Condition 2: the defect is in what this revision does when it is itself re-run, which no later revision can change. The correction keeps any table carrying its v2 key (`policy_uuid`, `claim_id`) whenever a v2 insurance table holds rows.

  **`6b2c3d4e5f6a` qualifies.** Condition 1: it dropped `issues.class_label` on bare existence. `a1c4e7d92f30` re-adds that name as the class name frozen at submission, which DOM-SUP-001 §VI says "must not be re-fetched live from ClassEconomy after submission". A re-application dropped every frozen label, and no lawful path can recover them. Condition 2: as above. The correction skips the drop only when `issues` no longer carries `class_id` and holds rows.

  **`0001_bootstrap` does not qualify, on condition 1.** Its replay drops `store_products` and `store_item_visibility` only when they hold no rows. When `store_products` holds rows it raises, and because the upgrade runs in one transaction (`migrations/env.py`), nothing is dropped. This was run against the merged revision over a migrated database.
